# ScAI Handover

Architecture, module map, and developer guide for contributors. For end-user workflow, see the [User Guide](user-guide.md); for installation and the project overview, see the [README](../README.md).

**Version**: 0.39.36 · **Last updated**: 2026-08-24

## 1. Runtime Architecture

ScAI is a React/Vite frontend backed by FastAPI, LangGraph, and local Jupyter execution. REST handles configuration and lifecycle operations; WebSockets stream workflow and report progress. Long-running workflows are background asyncio tasks, active analysis state is cached in process, and durable session artifacts are written to disk.

The workflow implementation is split across mixins under `backend/workflow/`. `SingleCellAnalysisWorkflow` in `core.py` composes routing, context, messaging, callbacks, annotation, planning, agent nodes, post-execution review, synthesis, initialization, graph construction, reporting, and integration behavior. The LangGraph internal entry node is `restore_entry`; ordinary runs route from it to `initialize_round`. It is an implementation detail used to select the correct continuation path, not a user-facing workflow phase.

`WORKFLOW_GRAPH_NODES` in `backend/constants.py` is the authoritative registry for the 16 graph nodes: `restore_entry`, `initialize_round`, `pi_planning`, `planning_dialogue`, `user_review_gate`, `biochemistry_analysis`, `pathology_analysis`, `bioinformatics_analysis`, `literature_research`, `pi_synthesis`, `wait_for_user_input`, `check_convergence`, `integration_synthesis`, `generate_final_report`, `generate_graphic_abstract`, and `generate_detailed_report`. The principal route is planning, optional modality analyses, Bioinformatics Stage 1/2, literature grounding, PI synthesis, user feedback, and convergence routing. Conditional edges can return a plan for revision, begin another round, enter integration, or proceed to reporting. Planning failure can terminate directly at `END`; a blocked final report can also terminate before graphic-abstract and detailed-report generation.

## 2. Repository Layout

```
scai/
├── backend/            FastAPI app, agents, workflow engine
├── frontend/           React + Vite UI
├── clients/python/     Python SDK (scai_client)
├── docs/               User, administrator, and technical documentation
├── Dockerfile          Multi-stage build image
├── docker-entrypoint.sh Container startup
├── start.sh / stop.sh  Native launch / shutdown
├── build.sh            Build helper
├── requirements.txt    Python dependencies
└── LICENSE             Apache 2.0
```

## 3. Backend Module Map (`backend/`)

| Path | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `main.py` | FastAPI app setup, middleware, lifespan tasks, and registration of 19 mounted routers. |
| `constants.py` | Application version, graph-node registry, node-to-agent mappings, workflow signals, and shared enums. |
| `workflow/` | LangGraph orchestration split into the 13 mixins composed by `core.py`; `graph.py` declares nodes and edges. |
| `agents/` | PI, Bioinformatics, Pathology, Biochemistry, Literature, Painter, Supervisor, and Technique implementations, with large agents split behind stable facades. |
| `routes/` | HTTP and WebSocket boundary modules; several large routers are split into sibling `*_parts/` packages. |
| `services/` | Session persistence, workflow task ownership, upload, cleanup, report generation, and other business services. |
| `models/` | Pydantic request, configuration, user, and analysis-state models; large schemas are split under `schema_parts/`. |
| `utils/` | LLM protocols, configuration resolution, Jupyter sandbox, authentication, storage, logging, and shared helpers. |
| `state.py` | Process-local session, workflow, task, lock, WebSocket, chat, report, and import-job registries. |
| `tests/` | Pytest suite, shared fixtures, structural guards, and pinned route registry. |

## 5. Configuration and Agents

Agent settings resolve through `AgentSettingsProvider` in three layers: global `SystemConfig` defaults, per-user credentials resolved by `config_resolver.py`, and per-session overrides stored on `AnalysisState`. Agent code must use the provider rather than reading global configuration directly; live session changes are visible on the next access.

The core agents are PI, Bioinformatics, and Literature. A missing model for any core agent enters a user intervention gate. Pathology, Biochemistry, Painter, Supervisor, and Technique are optional and can be disabled when not needed. Most agents derive from `BaseAgent`; `SupervisorAgent` deliberately has a parallel interface and is not a subclass.

Bioinformatics execution uses Stage 1 for broad QC, clustering, and annotation, followed by Stage 2 downstream analysis. Later rounds pursue approved directions, and the integration phase correlates completed directions before reporting. HITL gates cover plan review, annotation confirmation, direction selection, round guidance, and recoverable agent failures.

| Path | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `main.tsx`, `App.tsx` | React entry, error boundary, application shell, and top-level lifecycle. |

| `hooks/useApi.ts`, `hooks/api/` | Stable API facade plus split REST, session, configuration, WebSocket, and lifecycle hooks. |
| `stores/appStore.ts`, `stores/` | Stable Zustand facade plus split domain state, persistence, migrations, and store composition. |
| `i18n/translations.ts`, `i18n/` | Stable translation facade plus English, Chinese, and prompt modules. |
| `pages/` | Authentication, setup, administration, and configuration pages. |
| `utils/`, `constants/`, `types.ts` | Shared transformations, browser storage, download helpers, constants, and TypeScript interfaces. |

## 7. Data & State

| Where                                   | What                                                                                                           |
|-----------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| `backend/state.py` (in-process)         | Process-local registries for sessions, workflows, analysis/chat/report tasks, WebSockets, exports/imports, locks, access times, and internal checkpoint jobs.              |
| Per-session disk directory | `session.json`, workspace files, uploads, state/checkpoint data, reports, and logs. |
| SQLite | Users/auth, encrypted user LLM configuration, and application metadata. |
| Frontend `localStorage` | Versioned Zustand UI persistence and JWT storage; server state is reloaded after migration. |

**Session lifecycle**: in-memory state is authoritative while active. `session_store.py` performs throttled disk writes and LRU eviction, while per-session locks serialize mutations. Portable export/import includes session data and generated artifacts; runtime workflow state remains managed by backend lifecycle services.

### Kernel filesystem boundary

The Jupyter subprocess is launched through the trusted absolute launcher in
`backend/utils/kernel_startup/launcher.py`. On Linux, a first Python process
runs with `-I -S -B`, installs a Landlock ruleset before importing IPython or
workspace code, and then starts the kernel interpreter with `-P` and the
trusted startup directory. Kernel startup fails closed unless Landlock ABI 3
or newer is available and every rule can be installed; a workspace-provided
`sitecustomize.py` cannot run before the boundary.

The native boundary permits content writes, file creation/removal,
rename/link, and truncation only below three exact per-session roots: the
workspace, the dill state directory, and the private kernel temporary
directory. Cache and home variables are redirected into that temporary root;
allowed roots must exist, must not be symlinks, and must not resolve to `/`.
The enclosing session directory remains kernel-read-only, protecting
`session.json`, uploads, reports, Snapshot V2 objects, and journals. Runtime
snapshot material is first written to `states/.kernel-staging`, which the host
validates before performing the authoritative object-store commit.

Python wrappers and the audit hook produce actionable errors and cover normal
Python metadata mutations, while Landlock constrains native SQLite, HDF5, and
similar extension I/O. The scope is intentionally narrower than full process
isolation: reads and network access are not restricted, Landlock cannot mediate
every native metadata syscall, non-Linux systems have only best-effort Python
guards, and the state directory remains writable because dill persistence is
generated there. Do not represent this boundary as host isolation or as a
confidential-data sandbox.

The first-stage launcher must perform one trusted `exec` to enter ipykernel.
Only after that transition, the second interpreter's trusted
`sitecustomize.py` installs an irreversible CPython audit hook that rejects
`subprocess.Popen`, `os.system`, fork/forkpty, exec, and posix-spawn events
before IPython or workspace code loads. Local subprocesses and process pools
are therefore unavailable to analysis code; use threads or a remote scheduler.
Joblib/scikit-learn may silently fall back to a sequential backend when given
`n_jobs>1`. This Python audit layer prevents ordinary Python and external-tool
metadata bypasses, but it does not turn the kernel into a hostile-code sandbox:
native FFI can bypass Python audit events, so the native-metadata caveat above
remains load-bearing.

The container-wide `/dev/shm` is not writable from a kernel: exposing it would
give sessions a shared semaphore and shared-memory namespace. Consequently,
local `distributed.LocalCluster` construction is normalized to
`processes=False` and `Worker` threads even when callers request `Nanny`
processes; clients that connect to a remote Dask scheduler are not changed.
`DASK_DATAFRAME__QUERY_PLANNING=false` keeps arboreto 0.1.x on the compatible
legacy dataframe implementation. PyTorch `DataLoader` works with
`num_workers=0` and rejects larger values immediately with an actionable
message. These are deliberate capability restrictions of the per-session
filesystem boundary, not transient dependency failures.

## 8. Snapshot V2 (in development — not stable)

> **Status: in development, disabled by default, not stable.** The feature is off
> unless an operator sets `SCAI_ENABLE_SNAPSHOT_V2=true`, and the UI marks it
> experimental when it is on. On-disk layout, manifest schema and API shapes may
> change without a migration path, and the end-to-end behaviour has not been
> accepted on a real deployment — only under test, including real-kernel
> capture/restore acceptance. Treat stored snapshots as disposable: do not rely
> on one taken by an earlier build still restoring under a later one, and do not
> present the feature to end users. It is intentionally absent from the user
> guide.

Snapshot V2 captures a session's analysis state, notebook, workspace files,
LangGraph checkpoint and — when a kernel is live — the Jupyter runtime, so a
session can be returned to an earlier point of its analysis. It replaces the V1
checkpoint mechanism, whose restore path now answers HTTP 410; V1 records remain
listable and deletable for cleanup only.

### Storage model

Everything lives under the session directory, laid out by
`backend/services/snapshot_v2/paths.py`:

```
sessions/<id>/v2/          Snapshot V2 root
├── objects/               Content-addressed blobs, named by sha256
├── committed/             Published manifests (one JSON per snapshot), HMAC-signed
├── staging/               In-flight capture and restore working area
├── journals/              Transaction intent records (capture/restore/import/export/delete)
└── integrity/             Signature and evidence material
```

Objects are content-addressed, so an unchanged AnnData is stored once and shared
by every snapshot that references it — what grows the store is genuinely new
data, not the number of snapshots. A manifest names the objects a snapshot
consists of and carries a `root_hash` over its own canonical form; both the
manifest and the integrity material are HMAC-signed with the installation key.

### Lifecycle

| Operation | Entry point | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Capture | `SnapshotService.capture` (`service.py`, steps in `service_parts/capture_steps.py`) | Required captures at workflow boundaries are fail-closed; auto-captures are best-effort |
| Restore | `RestoreJobManager` → `RestoreCoordinator.apply` (`jobs.py`, `restore_parts/`) | Runs as a background job with a safe-point handshake; a pre_restore snapshot is taken first so the restore itself is undoable |
| Rollback | `recovery.py` `_RecoveryRestorer.rollback` | Reinstates the pre_restore image when a restore fails past its point of no return |
| Crash recovery | `recover_snapshot_transactions` (`recovery.py`), run at startup and on session load | Drives every non-terminal journal to a terminal state, or blocks the session |
| GC | `SnapshotGarbageCollector` (`gc.py`) | Mark-and-sweep over reachable objects. Runs on delete/import/recovery — **not** on a timer |
| Export / import | `exporter.py`, `importer.py` | Snapshots travel inside portable session archives; import defaults to `trust_runtime=false` |

### Concurrency and the safe point

Capture, restore, chat delegation and report generation can all be in flight
against one session, so mutating operations fence through a generation-scoped
cooperative safe point (`safe_point.py`). A capture or restore *requests* the
safe point; the workflow *acknowledges* it at a quiescent moment — a graph node
entry, a step boundary in the bioinformatics loop, or inside a HITL gate via the
gate's `safe_point_hook`. A busy owner surfaces as `SAFE_POINT_BUSY` → HTTP 409.
A restore that commits marks the superseded run, which unwinds with
`WorkflowSupersededError` rather than writing its stale position back.

### Continuation capsules

A snapshot records *where the workflow was*, not just its data. The graph's entry
node is `restore_entry`, and `build_restore_entry_plan` / `_route_from_restore_entry`
use the capsule to resume: `IdleCapsule` for a session with no live position,
`SemanticGateCapsule` for one parked at a resumable HITL gate, and a graph-node
capsule for a mid-round position. Do not assume a run always begins at
`initialize_round`.

Accepted actions at the five frozen semantic gates are converted by
`prepare_restored_semantic_action()` through `SEMANTIC_GATE_ACTION_NEXT_NODES`
to their exact post-action graph nodes before reconnect. `round_wait`,
`direction_selection`, and `integration_selection` continue at
`check_convergence`; `annotation_confirmation` continues at
`bioinformatics_analysis`; and `plan_revision` continues at
`planning_dialogue`. The parked gate is not replayed, and there is no
normal-versus-dedicated resume split.

### Capacity

Two ceilings, both checked before a capture writes and again after it lands: a
per-session store quota (`SCAI_SNAPSHOT_SESSION_QUOTA_GB`, default 500 GiB) and a
data-volume free-space reserve (`SCAI_SNAPSHOT_FREE_SPACE_RESERVE_GB`, default 50
GiB). The quota is per session, so the worst case is the quota times the number
of sessions holding snapshots; the reserve is the only volume-wide bound. The
snapshot panel advises a cleanup at 90% of the quota. See the
[Administrator Manual](admin-manual.md) for operator guidance.

A third ceiling is memory, not disk. `_SAVE_ANNDATA_TEMPLATE` deep-copies each
AnnData before sanitizing and writing it, and the dill path deserializes a second
copy to verify the round-trip, so a capture transiently needs roughly the size of
its largest captured object on top of what the kernel already holds. The kernel
introspection pass therefore reports each AnnData's true byte size (walking
X/layers/obsp/varp/obsm/varm plus `.raw`, counting sparse data/indices/indptr and
de-duplicating shared buffers), its own RSS, and the tighter of host
`MemAvailable` and the cgroup remainder. `_preflight_capture_memory()` refuses
before allocating when the largest copy would exceed
`SCAI_SNAPSHOT_MEMORY_SAFETY_RATIO` (default 0.8) of that headroom, raising
`snapshot_memory_insufficient`. Objects are captured one at a time, so the bound
is the largest single object, not their sum.

That gate offers `proceed` / `skip` / `terminate` and deliberately omits `retry`:
the preflight is deterministic, so a retry would refuse again and the gate could
never clear. `proceed` arms a one-shot
`AnalysisState.force_snapshot_memory_override` that the next capture consumes.
Missing evidence never refuses — an unreadable probe or unknown headroom falls
back to capturing. This is the preflight for Snapshot V2's own copy allocation.
Regular runtime persistence separately checks namespace count, largest object,
AnnData total, kernel RSS, estimated pickle size, and save time at persistence
boundaries. Those budgets default to automatic fractions of the container
cgroup memory limit (host memory fallback) and may be capped with deployment
environment variables. With Snapshot V2 disabled only its copy-specific probe
is absent. Arbitrary analysis allocations can still occur before the next
persistence boundary, so the container memory limit remains the hard backstop.

### Known limits

- **Not stable.** Layout, schema and API may change without migration.
- **No object alias identity across restore.** Supported top-level values and
  workflow continuation are preserved, but the same AnnData embedded inside an
  arbitrary nested container is not guaranteed to be the *same object* after a
  restore. Code must not rely on identity checks such as `nested["adata"] is adata`.
- **GC is event-driven, not scheduled.** Space from superseded objects is
  reclaimed when snapshots are deleted.
- **No cross-session total.** Only the per-session quota and the volume reserve
  bound disk use.
- **Not accepted end to end on a real deployment.** Coverage is automated,
  including real-kernel capture/restore acceptance and a fault-barrier matrix,
  but no operator acceptance run has been recorded.

## 9. Request & Data Flow

1. **Config** — first-run dialog posts API keys to `/api/config`; backend validates and persists.
2. **Session create** — `POST /api/session/start` creates and persists the initial session state; `GET /api/sessions` lists sessions.
3. **Group + data upload** — biochemistry indicators via `/api/session/.../indicators`; files via chunked `/api/session/.../upload*`.
4. **Start analysis** — `POST /api/session/.../start` schedules the workflow task; WebSocket `/ws/{id}` streams agent messages, figures, status.
5. **HITL gates** — plan review, annotation confirmation, direction selection, and round feedback are submitted through interaction/lifecycle routes while the workflow waits.
6. **Report** — report routes launch generation and stream progress; final HTML and supporting artifacts are persisted under the session.
7. **Export / import** — tarball / zip created in the background, tracked as a job; import reverses the process.

## 10. Reports and Operations

Two detailed-report generators must remain aligned: `backend/agents/supervisor_agent.py` is the live generation path, while `backend/services/report/generator.py` is the fallback. Shared report services provide planning, sections, figures, rendering, scoring, variants, and regeneration. Exploration and integrated reports use the workflow/report variants alongside the detailed HTML report.

Publication figures pass two reviewers of different standing before a report can carry them. The vision reviewers *judge*: PI `review_figure` (`agents/pi/figures.py`) is the blocking one, while `SupervisorAgent` evaluates any step that produced a figure and is advisory only — its verdict appends a `[Supervisor]` message and cannot reject anything, because `force_skip` derives from `code_error` alone and a figure that rendered has none. The linter *measures*: `services/figure_layout.py` supplies source injected into the publication-figure wrapper, which draws the figure inside the kernel before `savefig`, asks the renderer for every text artist's window extent, and prints one `__SCAI_LAYOUT_LINT__` line. It runs in the kernel because only the kernel holds a live `Figure`; the backend ever sees JPEG bytes. Every violation carries its own `severity`: `text_over_text` and `text_over_foreign_axes` can block or advise according to what collided, `tick_label_collision` blocks, and `tiny_text` advises. There is deliberately no out-of-canvas check because every report save uses `bbox_inches='tight'`. Consumers must read `severity`, never infer it from `kind`. A blocking violation is not overridable by either reviewer while revision budget remains, and `is_report_eligible` rejects it regardless of `review_status`. An absent verdict means unverified, not clean — `lint_ran()` distinguishes them.

A measurement or serialization failure emits `__SCAI_LAYOUT_LINT_ERROR__` without a normal verdict. The backend parser represents absent or malformed payloads as `[]`, but `lint_ran()` accepts only a valid list-shaped verdict. An unmeasured first render is persisted as `lint_skipped`; an unmeasured revision retains the previous valid measurement, and the renderer itself uses the same marker when its comparison safety cap skips a complete measurement. A diagnostic artifact waives only reviewer status and only when `layout_was_measured()` is true. Gallery and archival surfaces share `is_report_eligible()` and `published_head_artifacts()`; audit-only attempts cannot become current gallery entries merely because their files remain on disk.

Renderer verdict dependencies are captured once in the bootstrap-owned
`scai_kernel_trust` module rather than in IPython's mutable `user_ns`.
`sitecustomize.py` preloads that module before the workspace enters `sys.path`,
so an old workspace file cannot shadow it. A wrapper re-parents the pristine
ipykernel `OutStream` to the current execute request before user code, restores
shared plotting/output functions afterward, and emits the validated lint event
as its final output line. This ordering prevents prior-cell changes to
`sys.stdout`, `json.dumps`, `builtins.print`, or Matplotlib helpers from turning
a measured collision into an apparently clean verdict or routing it to an old
request.

Docker and direct-run deployment are supported. For ports, volumes, environment variables, build flags, reverse proxy setup, user administration, backup, and troubleshooting, use the [Administrator Manual](admin-manual.md) or [管理员手册](admin-manual-zh.md) rather than duplicating operational parameters here.

## 11. Development Guide

- **Dev loop**: `./start.sh --dev` — hot reload for both frontend and backend.
- **Supported Python tests**: use the immutable runner described in [Test Strategy](test-strategy.md):
  `scripts/scai-test fast backend/tests/test_x.py::test_y`,
  `scripts/scai-test client`, `scripts/scai-test kernel`,
  `scripts/scai-test backend-full`, or
  `scripts/scai-test -- <raw pytest args>`.
- **Supported frontend tests**: `scripts/scai-frontend-test --install-and-test` at the separately authorized isolated npm checkpoint.
- **Test evidence**: every supported run records its image, source, command, counts, and artifact evidence under `test-results/`; focused evidence is not full-suite or release evidence.
- **Adding a route**: create `backend/routes/<name>.py`, register it in `main.py`, update `route_snapshot.json` (`test_route_registry.py` will tell you if it drifts).
- **Adding an agent**: subclass `backend/agents/base.py` where appropriate, register settings, and wire the node through `backend/workflow/` and `backend/constants.py`.
- **Adding a workflow node**: update the relevant mixin, `workflow/graph.py`, `WORKFLOW_GRAPH_NODES`, node mappings, and workflow state.
- **Adding a report section**: update report services and keep both detailed-report generators in sync.
- **Conventions**: Pydantic models validate at route boundaries; services contain logic; agents are thin LLM orchestrators; `utils/` holds cross-cutting helpers; config is centralized in `backend/config/` with `PI_AGENT_NAME` as the single source of truth for the PI identifier.

## 12. Python Client SDK (`clients/python/`)

`scai_client` provides a `Client` and job-oriented batch API for submitting analysis configurations, waiting for completion, and downloading exports. Its package version follows the ScAI application release. See the [Python client README](../clients/python/README.md) for the authoritative API and examples.

## 13. Version History (condensed)

Detailed release notes for every version are available through `git log` and release tags. For versions before 0.30.1, changes are summarized by major version group; from 0.30.1 onward, each version is documented individually. The summary below highlights the architectural arc of the project.

**0.39.x — (2026-08-04 onward)**

- `0.39.36` — Production Docker contexts exclude repository-local `test-results/`, preventing ignored test evidence, isolated npm installations, and caches from being copied into `/app` or traversed by the historical recursive ownership layer. A static regression pins the exclusion rule.
- `0.39.35` — Restored production frontend builds by keeping the reconnect ownership predicate in scope for both the reload path and its error handler, and by giving the deferred fetch mock the browser fetch signature. The affected WebSocket suite passed `10/10`, the complete frontend suite passed `347/347` in 55 files, and `tsc && vite build` completed in an isolated Node 20.19.2 container. Backend, Python client, and kernel suites were not run because the repair is frontend-only.
- `0.39.34` — Final gate-rebaseline closure: tracker-known disposable globals are reclaimed once under namespace pressure without deleting required, canonical, or AnnData state; current Python-client documentation now matches the application-following version contract. Frozen source digest `dc92142cbc5c9f48a72b3ef9c89e4d75a5659a35ae6da9fa44a460ebe974fd54` produced complete collection `5790`, backend-full `5750 collected / 5577 passed / 79 failed / 94 skipped`, frontend `347/347` in 145 suites, client `40/40`, and kernel `282 collected / 252 passed / 28 failed / 2 errors`. Backend and kernel failed/error node sets exactly match their accepted X2 and S4 baselines, so both deltas are zero. Evidence: `test-results/20260824T080624Z-292980/evidence.json`, `test-results/20260824T080702Z-297474/evidence.json`, `test-results/20260824T081338Z-b4ed00a8e28b-frontend/evidence.json`, `test-results/20260824T081422Z-326045/evidence.json`, and `test-results/20260824T081447Z-329867/evidence.json`.
- `0.39.33` — X3 independent frontend UX: persisted accessible sidebar resizing/collapse, server-authoritative startup session selection, selector-level New Analysis, unified PNG branding, and browser-authoritative system theme with light publication canvases. The frozen frontend checkpoint passed `347/347` in 145 suites at `test-results/20260824T073521Z-390604d21782-frontend/evidence.json`. The production build retained three pre-existing TypeScript errors in unchanged `useWebSocket` source/tests; backend-full, client, and kernel were not run.
- `0.39.32` — X2 cache telemetry and stable prompt prefixes: Chat Completions, Responses and Anthropic cache metrics now distinguish absent/null/default values from explicit zero, Anthropic input accounting includes cache creation/read tokens, and figure revision keeps invariant instructions ahead of variable context without changing request behavior. The frozen backend-full checkpoint recorded `5749 collected / 5576 passed / 79 failed / 94 skipped` at `test-results/20260824T063715Z-20266/evidence.json`, with no failed-node delta from X1. Frontend, client, and kernel were not run.
- `0.39.31` — X1 pathology coverage observation and disclosure: bounded per-image tile coverage and diagnostics now cross round state, persistence/API, WebSocket summaries, report prompts and rendered limitations, while reload preserves the wire record and scalar review instructions remain bounded. The frozen backend-full checkpoint recorded `5745 collected / 5572 passed / 79 failed / 94 skipped` at `test-results/20260824T061118Z-4105133/evidence.json`, with no failed-node delta from A3; the frozen frontend checkpoint passed `333/333` at `test-results/20260824T061105Z-547d3b54c048-frontend/evidence.json`. Client and kernel were not run.
- `0.39.30` — A3 model-output shape and bounded planning tolerance: closed renderer aliases, shared scalar/mapping/list/null normalization, reserved parser-sentinel handling, one bounded schema repair, typed plan-generation failure, and last-valid-seed fallback without new planning routes or gates. The frozen backend-full checkpoint recorded `5737 collected / 5564 passed / 79 failed / 94 skipped` at `test-results/20260824T053052Z-3938286/evidence.json`; all 79 failures match the accepted A2 baseline, leaving no A3 failed-node delta. Frontend, client, and kernel were not run.
- `0.39.29` — A2 bounded agent work and failure handling: bounded PubMed work and cache reuse, explicit biochemistry image states, typed tool-protocol failures, advisory cycle repair, normalized supervisor evaluation, domain-neutral API guidance, and complete upload inventory propagation. - `0.39.19` — F4 notebook/state durability rebaseline: descriptor-pinned notebook media, bounded state size and checkpoint retention, copy-only durable realtime history, and verified standalone snapshots (2026-08-23).
- `0.39.18` — F3 ordinary dill runtime-save rebaseline: atomic, bounded, cancellation-safe save generations that preserve the prior valid generation on failure (2026-08-23).
- `0.39.17` — F2 export and secret-boundary rebaseline: descriptor-owned archive staging with exact range cleanup, authenticated state-mutating report approval and credential isolation, and frontend test evidence anchored to the verified run-start HEAD (2026-08-23).

- `0.39.16` — F1 kernel lifecycle rebaseline: core-dump suppression and signal diagnosis, bounded native asynchronous Jupyter lifecycle operations, and run-start source-evidence identity (2026-08-23):
  - **Kernel failures remain bounded and diagnosable**: the trusted launcher suppresses core dumps by default with `RLIMIT_CORE=(0, 0)` while preserving an explicit host-developer opt-in; kernel-death reports distinguish ordinary exits and fatal signals before restart replaces the process identity.
  - **Jupyter lifecycle calls use bounded native async operations**: start, restart, and shutdown keep the event loop responsive; timeout, error, and cancellation paths clean partial resources, while unsuccessful shutdown retains the same manager/process for an explicit retry.
  - **Test evidence is anchored once**: `scripts/scai-test` captures the verified run-start HEAD once and reuses it for pre-run, post-run, and published evidence without weakening source-drift detection.
  - **Verification**: kernel run `20260823T031849Z-2848414` recorded `214 collected / 185 passed / 29 failed / 0 skipped`; the 29 failure-node set exactly matches F0 run `20260823T014807Z-2515509`, and every F1 node passed.

- `0.39.15` — F0 reproducible-environment rebaseline: Python 3.11 direct-start identity, scientific dependency bounds, immutable hash-locked Python test image, repository-owned serial profiles, isolated locked frontend tests, and content-addressed evidence (2026-08-22):
  - **Direct startup is fail-closed (`start.sh`, dependency inputs)**: system or uv-managed Python is explicitly verified as minor 3.11, stale versioned venvs are refused without deletion, and failed pip or required npm installation starts no service.
  - **Python test dependencies are immutable (`docker/test-deps*`, `scripts/lock-test-deps.sh`, image build/verification scripts)**: Python 3.11.15/linux-amd64 and every artifact are hash locked; application source and production dependency volumes are absent.
  - **Test claims are content-addressed (`scripts/scai-test`, `scripts/scai-frontend-test`, `test-profiles.toml`, `docs/test-strategy.md`)**: every run records immutable dependency identity, exact source bytes, commands, environment, counts, duration, and claim scope; focused evidence is never release proof.
  - **Verification is recorded after execution (`docs/coding/gate-rebaseline-f0-baseline.md`)**: the tracked record is added only after the final candidate matrix and describes failures without changing unrelated application behavior.

- `0.39.1` — Runtime persistence capacity is now deployment-sized instead of being constrained by the fixed 4/12/16/8 GiB object, AnnData, RSS, and runtime-file defaults introduced with the persistence failure gate. This keeps the fail-closed verified-generation boundary while allowing the same platform build to operate on small Docker allocations, large-memory servers, and analyses ranging from tens of thousands to approximately one million cells without source edits (2026-08-04):
  - **Memory budgets default to the effective deployment capacity (`backend/config/_config.py`, `backend/utils/runtime_budget.py`)**: the four fixed byte ceilings now default to automatic mode. Resolution reads cgroup v2 `memory.max`, then cgroup v1 `memory.limit_in_bytes`, and falls back to host `MemTotal`. Effective limits are 25% for the largest object, 45% for total live AnnData, 60% for kernel RSS, and 30% for the compressed runtime file. The namespace ceiling and save timeout remain independently bounded.
  - **Administrators can install deployment-only caps without persisting machine policy into application configuration (`backend/utils/runtime_budget.py`, `backend/workflow/core.py`)**: `SCAI_RUNTIME_MAX_SINGLE_OBJECT_GB`, `SCAI_RUNTIME_MAX_ANNDATA_TOTAL_GB`, `SCAI_RUNTIME_MAX_RSS_GB`, and `SCAI_RUNTIME_MAX_PKL_GB` accept positive GiB values or `auto`; namespace count and save timeout have their own positive environment controls. Numeric capacity values are upper bounds and are still clamped to the automatic fraction. Workflow startup logs both configured and effective budgets with the detected capacity source.
  - **Upgrades cannot silently resurrect the former 16 GiB ceiling (`backend/utils/secret_redaction.py`)**: all six deployment-only runtime fields are stripped on both global-config sealing and opening. New database writes omit them, while legacy `app_meta` payloads are ignored at load time even when they already contain encrypted credentials and therefore need no credential migration.
  - **Operator guidance covers large and concurrent deployments (`docs/admin-manual.md`, `docs/admin-manual-zh.md`)**: both manuals document automatic resolution, every environment override, explicit profiles through approximately one million cells, and an extrapolated 100-sample / one-million-cell sizing row. They distinguish per-session persistence budgets from global concurrency scheduling and clarify that disk checkpoints do not evict live Python objects, reduce kernel RSS, or turn backed AnnData into an out-of-core execution guarantee.
  - **Snapshot scope documentation is corrected (`AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/HANDOVER.md`)**: disabling Snapshot V2 removes only the copy-specific capture preflight. Regular persistence budgets still run at persistence boundaries, while the container memory limit remains the hard backstop for arbitrary allocations between those boundaries. No dense-matrix, AnnData lifecycle, or out-of-core analysis algorithm was changed in this release.
  - **Verification**: 119 targeted backend tests cover automatic and explicit budgets, the 2 TiB capacity case, invalid environment values, legacy-config stripping, runtime-persistence gates, snapshot-failure resume, workflow close, secret persistence, configuration I/O, and version consistency. The Python sources compile and `git diff --check` is clean.

**0.38.x — (2026-08-01 onward)**

- `0.38.13` — Marker-database ORA priors are now an explicit, observable evidence path instead of a silent best-effort side channel. Previously, `_compute_database_priors` returned an opaque `dict` on success and `{}` on every failure (unsupported organism, sandbox crash, bad JSON, empty result), making it impossible to tell whether the annotation proceeded with database support, without it by choice, or without it by accident. The ORA kernel also silently disappeared when the Scanpy rank key was unavailable — a legitimate state when `annotate_clusters` uses explicitly supplied descriptive markers — so the database evidence vanished from the annotation prompt without trace. Both defects are repaired (2026-08-03):
  - **`DatabasePriorResult` makes every outcome explicit (`backend/agents/bioinformatics_agent.py`)**: a frozen dataclass with `status` (`used` / `empty` / `failed` / `skipped`), `priors` dict, `detail` string, and `marker_source`. `summary()` and `realtime_message()` produce human-readable strings for the analysis log and WebSocket status. `_compute_database_priors` now returns `DatabasePriorResult` instead of `dict`: sandbox execution failure, missing JSON output, parse errors, and type mismatches all return `status="failed"` with a compacted detail string instead of silently degrading to `{}`. An empty but successful computation returns `status="empty"` rather than being indistinguishable from a failure. The marker-DB HITL gate's SKIP action records `status="skipped"` with "user chose literature-only annotation," and an unavailable database or workspace records its own skip reason. The status notification now reports the actual outcome ("Marker database priors used: …" / "computed but no eligible matches" / "failed: …" / "skipped: …") instead of the generic "Annotating N clusters using literature evidence."
  - **ORA kernel falls back to explicitly supplied cluster markers when the rank key is absent (`backend/services/ora_kernel.py`)**: `compute_ora_priors` accepts an optional `cluster_markers` dict. When `sc.get.rank_genes_groups_df` raises (e.g. the key was never computed), the function falls back to the sanitized marker mapping that `annotate_clusters` is actually using, rather than silently returning nothing. Each hit record carries a `marker_source` field (`"rank_genes_groups"` or `"provided_cluster_markers"`) so the annotation prompt and audit log can distinguish the evidence source. Clusters not present in `adata.obs[cluster_key]` are excluded from the fallback. When both the rank key and explicit markers are unavailable, the original exception propagates rather than being silenced.
  - **Cluster markers are forwarded to the ORA computation (`backend/agents/bioinformatics_agent.py`)**: `_compute_database_priors` now accepts and serializes `cluster_markers` into the sandbox call, so the kernel-side fallback has the same evidence the agent is using for annotation.
  - **Annotation step records carry the prior summary (`backend/agents/bioinformatics_agent.py`)**: both the intermediate and final annotation `AnalysisStep` entries include `annotation_database_priors_summary` from `DatabasePriorResult.summary()`, replacing the previous call to `summarize_database_priors()` that could silently produce an empty string on failure.
  - **Verification**: 5 new/extended backend tests cover sandbox failure surfacing, successful-empty distinction, explicit status values for log/realtime, rank-key fallback with provided markers, rank-key absence without markers raising rather than silencing, and database priors reaching the PI matching prompt. +292 / −29 across 5 files.

- `0.38.12` — A third audit pass closes the remaining failure windows around account deletion, upload integrity, publication downloads, supplementary code safety, report fallback, WebSocket delivery, and frontend session ownership. The changes are organized into nine themes, each addressing a distinct class of defect confirmed by executing the shipped code against the pre-change tree (2026-08-03):
  - **Account deletion is now a two-phase immediate-revocation lifecycle (`backend/models/user.py`, `middleware/auth.py`, `routes/admin.py`, `routes/auth.py`, `services/user_cleanup.py`, `utils/auth.py`, `utils/db.py`, `frontend/src/pages/Admin.tsx`, `frontend/src/stores/domain.ts`)**: a new `deleting` user status blocks all access (login, API, WebSocket) while physical session cleanup remains retryable. Previously, deletion set `pending`, conflating "awaiting approval" with "awaiting cleanup." Auth middleware and WebSocket authentication reject already-issued JWTs for `deleting` accounts while HTTP logout remains available. Admin mutations (status/role/password) use DB-level `unless_current_status` predicates and `required_admin_id` subqueries to prevent TOCTOU races. `mark_user_deleting_if_not_last_admin` is an atomic `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction that checks the last-usable-admin invariant before setting `deleting`, preventing concurrent cross-deletes from removing all admins. Self-service password change uses `compare_and_swap_user_password` — a CAS that checks the old hash and allowed statuses atomically. Frontend shows "Deletion in progress" badge with "Retry cleanup" button; role/status selects are read-only for deleting users. Upgrade caveat: pre-0.38.11 interrupted deletions may remain stored as `pending` and cannot be distinguished automatically from genuine awaiting-approval accounts.
  - **Upload chunk integrity is end-to-end size-bounded (`backend/services/upload.py`, `routes/upload.py`, `routes/v1.py`, `utils/db.py`)**: `total_size` column added to `upload_sessions` with migration for legacy rows (NULL → fail-closed). `init_upload` validates total_size range, chunk_size range, chunk geometry, and requires 64-char hex `expected_sha256`. `upload_chunk` validates each chunk's byte length against its expected index-based length. `complete_upload` validates per-chunk size during assembly, tracks `assembled_size`, rejects overflow, and verifies `assembled_size == total_size`. V1 multipart route uses `read_chunk_with_limit` instead of unbounded `file.read()`. Legacy upload sessions (pre-migration, `total_size IS NULL`) are rejected with 409 "restart upload."
  - **Publication figure downloads are path-confined and integrity-verified in one pass (`backend/services/report/artifacts.py`, `services/report/figure_discovery.py`, `routes/reports_parts/publication.py`, `services/session_store.py`)**: `resolve_verified_archival_artifact` and `resolve_verified_legacy_artifact` resolve, verify, and return `(bytes, Path)` in one call, replacing the old resolve-path-then-verify-bytes pattern. Path authorization is session-scoped: `path_is_session_owned()` checks against a whitelist of session-owned roots, preventing a malicious `png_path`/`pdf_path` from reading arbitrary files. `has_frozen_publication_pair` accepts a valid `preview_sha256` as a substitute for a damaged legacy `render_sha256`. `_write_preview_noclobber` uses `O_EXCL | O_NOFOLLOW` for symlink-safe, clobber-safe creation. `backfill_legacy_publication_integrity` recovers integrity hashes for pre-registry PublicationFigures from their frozen preview bytes; called during session load recovery. Session store adds `PAUSED` to `_FORCE_SAVE_STATUSES` so paused states bypass the save throttle.
  - **Supplementary code safety resolves import aliases and uses content-based quarantine (`backend/agents/bioinformatics/supplementary.py`)**: the static code gate now resolves `import`/`from ... import` aliases via `qualified_call_name()` and `module_aliases`/`symbol_aliases` dicts, catching `from os import open as o; o(...)` and `import scanpy.pl; scanpy.pl.umap(...)`. New `_SUPPLEMENTARY_EXACT_WRITE_CALLS` frozenset blocks `os.open`, `os.write`, `os.rename`, `shutil.copy`, etc. by qualified name. Image quarantine snapshots `(stem, size, mtime_ns, sha256_digest)` instead of `(stem, mtime_ns)`, so restoring mtime after content modification no longer evades detection.
  - **Deterministic report fallback when the report-writing model is unavailable (`backend/services/report/generator.py`, `services/report/planning.py`, `services/report/sections.py`, `workflow/reporting_parts/final_report.py`, `main.py`)**: `force_fallback=True` skips all LLM calls (PI review, section writing, abstract, conclusions) and produces deterministic text from retained workflow evidence. `_generate_abstract`/`_generate_conclusions` return canned summaries; `_generate_report_section` returns empty body. Report metadata includes `degraded_fallback: True`. Workflow on SKIP after LLM failure retries with `force_fallback=True`. `ReportGenerationError` handler in `main.py` returns 409 with `REPORT_GENERATION_INVALID` code instead of 500.
  - **WebSocket delivery is cancellation-resistant with shutdown drain (`backend/utils/websocket_delivery.py`, `main.py`)**: abandoned transport tasks are tracked and drained during app shutdown. `_await_task_with_hard_timeout` creates a separate `asyncio.Task` with a deadline, cancelling and tracking the task if it doesn't complete. `unregister_websocket(close=True)` spawns an independent close task that owns the lifecycle, so caller cancellation doesn't strand a logically-closed connection in the registry. `drain_abandoned_transport_tasks` cancels and bounded-waits all abandoned tasks with a 2s timeout during shutdown.
  - **Bulk I/O uses a separate thread pool (`backend/utils/asyncio_tasks.py`, `routes/reports_parts/downloads.py`, `routes/reports_parts/publication.py`)**: `run_in_owned_bulk_thread` with its own 4-worker `ThreadPoolExecutor` for long filesystem operations (zip building, image verification), preventing starvation of latency-sensitive owned-thread work (upload chunks, small reads). Publication downloads no longer hold the lifecycle lock during image decoding/hashing.
  - **Frontend session-ownership guards prevent stale cross-session mutations (`frontend/src/components/ChatPanel.tsx`, `hooks/api/useWebSocket.ts`)**: ChatPanel introduces `ChatRequestOwner` tracking with `(sessionId, requestId, controller)`; an `ownsRequest()` closure checks all identity fields before applying state mutations. Session-switch `useEffect` aborts the active request and clears loading/delegation state. useWebSocket adds `ownsSocketSession()` that checks both `isCurrentAttempt()` and session identity; `onclose` checks session identity before scheduling reconnects, preventing socket-A's close from reconnecting into session-B.
  - **Verification**: the repair matrix passed 52 non-overlapping backend tests across account deletion, upload integrity, publication downloads, supplementary safety, report fallback, WebSocket delivery, session store, and route contracts. Frontend passed 303 tests across 46 files. +3,376 / −507 across 52 files.

- `0.38.11` — A second transaction and concurrency audit closes the failure windows left around the generation ownership introduced in 0.38.10. Restore activation, user deletion, WebSocket replacement, chat uploads, report publication, runtime persistence, and frontend session switches now either commit one owned generation or fail without publishing a mixed one. All 12 issues confirmed by the consolidated follow-up review are repaired, Python sources compile, the Vite production build succeeds, and `git diff --check` is clean. A monolithic backend run remains unsuitable as release evidence on the current Python 3.13 host because `aiosqlite`/AnyIO executor shutdown and real-kernel tests can hang, so the release record uses the completed resource-bounded suites instead (2026-08-03):
  - **Snapshot restore activation is one recovery-protected transaction (`backend/services/snapshot_v2/`)**: post-apply continuation activation now remains inside the recovery barrier, restore reservations are published with their lifecycle generation before deletion can pass them, and dispatcher activation builds a detached candidate before swapping the live `AnalysisState`. An activation, figure-clone, provenance, persistence, or verification failure therefore recovery-blocks the generation instead of leaving restored disk state under stale live memory.
  - **Deletion and account cleanup cannot overlook work that is not yet in the ordinary session inventory (`backend/services/user_cleanup.py`, `session_deletion.py`, `middleware/auth.py`, import and lifecycle services)**: importing sessions, incomplete tombstones, reserved Snapshot jobs, pending task teardown, and same-ID reuse are all generation-scoped. Account deletion first places the account in the distinct, non-approvable `deleting` state; both HTTP middleware and WebSocket authentication reject already-issued JWTs for that durable state while HTTP logout remains available; API keys are revoked; cleanup attempts every owned generation without short-circuiting, records retryable pending cleanup, and returns success only after the owned data has been retired. Admin password changes and last-admin demotion use the same database status predicate as role/status changes, so no stale read can mutate a `deleting` row and deleting admins are excluded from the guarded demotion count. Upgrade caveat: pre-0.38.11 interrupted deletions may remain stored as `pending` and cannot be distinguished automatically from genuine awaiting-approval accounts. Operators must reconcile historical `pending` rows against cleanup evidence and retry Delete for confirmed interrupted deletions rather than approving or bulk-migrating them.
  - **Connection, chat, and frontend response ownership is exact (`backend/utils/websocket_delivery.py`, WebSocket/chat routes, `frontend/src/hooks/`, `ChatPanel.tsx`)**: an old WebSocket handler can no longer forward payloads to its replacement, send/close/retirement waits have hard deadlines even for cancellation-resistant transports, and a timed-out retirement attempts transport closure at most once before dropping its owner slot without falsely recording that the transport closed. Python cannot forcibly terminate a coroutine that permanently suppresses cancellation, so at most one send and one close task can remain attached to such a defective transport; this residual runtime limitation no longer blocks a replacement generation but may retain that transport until process exit. Chat upload uses no-clobber staging, symlink/inode checks, size limits, rollback, and a generation fence; delegation check-and-registration is atomic. Session history, polling, send success/failure/finalizers, and asynchronous delegation Stop all carry session/request/controller ownership guards.
  - **Runtime and LLM failures preserve durable, actionable state (`backend/workflow/core.py`, `backend/utils/sandbox.py`, `backend/utils/llm_client_parts/`)**: every successful transition to `paused` bypasses persistence throttling and reaches `session.json`; optional namespace objects are serialized independently so one unsupported third-party value cannot corrupt the whole runtime generation; duplicate intervention actions are first-writer-wins. Vision calls receive the agent system prompt, long-timeout requests retain their 429 backoff budget, public-answer recovery does not expose private reasoning, and Supervisor evaluations use a validated structured response with a bounded parse retry.
  - **Reports publish immutable generations rather than a mutable tree (`backend/services/report/output_generation.py`, report routes and services)**: JSON, HTML, figures, tables, and assets are assembled and validated in private staging, fsynced into an immutable generation, and exposed by one atomic current pointer. Readers, downloads, exports, imports, branch lineages, retention, and archive recovery follow that pointer and verified artifact identities; bulk ZIP endpoints are disk-backed and enforce file/count/size limits. Standalone reports honor the active restore branch, stalled provider cleanup has a deadline, and exhausted report generation persists a bounded-intervention fallback that becomes deterministic without a Supervisor as well as with one. Invalid published-generation layouts return a stable 409 contract rather than an internal 500.
  - **Figure evidence cannot inherit trust from a mutable path (`backend/services/report/artifacts.py`, `figure_contract.py`, supplementary and review code)**: a pre-registry record may recover the digest of its persisted preview because those bytes live inside the record; its current raster is adopted only when it is byte-identical to that preview, and a missing PDF digest is never inferred from a mutable path. Such a record remains unavailable to publication/report surfaces until a historically bound PDF exists or the figure is regenerated and reviewed. Preview recovery returns the already validated record bytes after a no-clobber write rather than reading, decoding, and hashing the recovery file again. Records at or after the registry cutover continue to fail closed when identity is missing. Render/PDF pairs are frozen together, lineage and round/current eligibility are applied consistently, and transitional same-plan figures remain matchable. Supplementary execution resolves imported write aliases and compares content digests, so restoring an overwritten image's mtime does not bypass quarantine.
  - **Bounded resource and compatibility fixes cover the remaining audit cases**: DB-backed V1 uploads persist the initialized `total_size`, enforce exact per-index chunk lengths, recheck the aggregate byte budget, and fail closed for pre-migration upload sessions whose accepted size is unknowable. Its multipart route now performs a sentinel-byte read capped at the global chunk ceiling before service validation, so an oversized part cannot allocate an unbounded in-memory `bytes` object. Legacy web chunked uploads validate every exact chunk length and the complete file against a required whole-file SHA-256. This is an intentional compatibility break: clients calling `POST /api/session/{session_id}/upload/init-chunked` must compute the file digest and send it as the 64-character hexadecimal `expected_sha256` field; clients predating 0.38.11 must be updated. The Python SDK already sends the field. Long report archive, publication scan, and image verification work uses a separate owned bulk executor so it cannot occupy the latency-sensitive persistence/upload pool. The Python SDK also treats durable `stopped` as terminal; sandbox IOPub draining and report cleanup have overall deadlines; statistical small-sample checks tolerate unknown group sizes; report and reproducibility downloads reject unsafe or excessive inputs; and cleanup/export/import paths preserve lifecycle ownership across cancellation and exceptions.
  - **Verification was layered around the changed contracts**: the follow-up repair matrix passed 232 non-overlapping backend tests in resource-safe groups (149 session/report/figure/fallback, 66 executor/publication/WebSocket/upload/exception, 12 compatibility/account-cleanup, and 5 route/version contracts). The C12 middleware review passed 25 authentication/middleware contract tests plus 4 deletion-state route/login regressions. The final review matrix passed 160 focused backend tests with 3 environment skips across publication migration/downloads, V1 chunk bounds, admin deletion races, and WebSocket authentication/delivery. The full frontend matrix passed 303 tests across 46 files and the production build succeeded; backend and Python client sources compiled successfully. Real-`AppDB` tests remain present for compatible CI/runtime environments, while this host's `aiosqlite 0.22.1` hangs at the minimal connection boundary before a test can execute; synchronous SQLite probes verified the new schema, legacy `NULL` migration, and all guarded `deleting` predicates here.

- `0.38.10` — Long-running work is now bound to an exact session generation from request entry through persistence, publication, delivery, and teardown. The three-container runtime audit exposed a common failure class behind otherwise unrelated symptoms: a cancelled coroutine, stale in-memory object, reused session ID, or path-shaped filename could outlive the operation that owned it and then overwrite, delete, publish, or resurrect another operation's state. This release adds durable ownership records and fail-closed transactions at those boundaries, hardens the kernel and report pipelines, and moves production to a single prebuilt SPA process. The complete backend corpus passed in resource-safe shards (`4,915 passed, 84 skipped`); the Python client passed 28 tests; the frontend passed 290 tests and built with Vite 8.2.0; the production launch contract passed all 7 tests (2026-08-03):
  - **Session deletion and same-ID reuse are generation transactions (`backend/services/session_lifecycle.py`, `session_deletion.py`, lifecycle/session routes)**: durable owner-scoped generation records and tombstones fence every mutation before any path is removed. One deletion path retires and drains workflows, analysis/chat/report tasks, WebSockets, imports, exports, kernels, snapshot jobs, caches, and per-session artifacts before a generation can be reused. Legacy state discovery now matches only exact supported suffixes, so deleting or exporting `abc` cannot consume the state of `abc_state`, `abc_session`, or another near-prefix session.
  - **Ordinary and chunked uploads, imports, and exports publish only operation-owned bytes (`backend/routes/upload.py`, `backend/services/upload.py`, `backend/services/export_lifecycle.py`)**: no-clobber staging, fsync boundaries, hard-link identity proofs, and memory/disk rollback replace direct writes to final paths. Cancellation and save failure cannot truncate a prior upload or leave an unrecorded file, including on CPython 3.13. Prepared exports carry a hard-link ownership marker tied to the archive inode; direct, TTL, and orphan cleanup refuse replacement or prefix-only candidates. Import and chunk assembly records remain owner/session/generation scoped and recoverable until state persistence commits.
  - **Async task and WebSocket ownership is explicit (`backend/utils/asyncio_tasks.py`, `websocket_delivery.py`, workflow runner and WebSocket routes)**: blocking resource calls run in dedicated executors that are drained and joined before cancellation propagates; ordered delivery is bound to the connection's event loop and generation. Report stop-summary tasks are registered before execution, so deletion can cancel and await them instead of allowing a completed run to recreate state after force-delete. The frontend rejects stale socket callbacks and closes the previous connection before reconnecting.
  - **Runtime persistence and Snapshot V2 fail closed (`backend/services/runtime_persistence_gate.py`, `backend/services/snapshot_v2/`, workflow continuation code)**: a failed final save preserves the live kernel and opens a retry/manage/terminate intervention instead of reporting a clean stop. Capture, restore, recovery, import/export, and deletion carry generation checks, durable transaction evidence, sanitized errors, safe-point reservations, and bounded runtime memory policy. Restored semantic actions map to their exact post-gate nodes, preventing a reconnect from replaying the parked gate or taking a stale branch. Snapshot V2 remains experimental and disabled by default.
  - **The Jupyter boundary is installed before workspace code (`backend/utils/kernel_startup/`, sandbox modules)**: the trusted absolute launcher installs a fail-closed Landlock write boundary on Linux before IPython and scientific imports, redirects writable cache/home/temp state per session, and then installs an irreversible process-launch audit hook. Workspace shadow modules cannot preempt trusted startup. Local Dask and joblib use thread/serial fallbacks, and PyTorch `DataLoader` rejects process workers because container-wide shared memory is deliberately outside the allowlist. Reads and network remain outside this boundary; it is not represented as full host or confidential-data isolation.
  - **Figures and reports commit as evidence-bound transactions (`backend/services/report/figure_transactions.py`, `figure_layout.py`, report services and agent figure paths)**: JPG, PDF, review metadata, and session state are journaled and recovered as one publication attempt. Only the current eligible head reaches galleries, reports, archives, and downloads; failed, abandoned, superseded, unmeasured, or layout-blocked attempts remain audit evidence rather than current output. Renderer capabilities live in a trusted bootstrap module, layout consumers honor each violation's recorded severity, and every report surface emits deterministic partial/failure and evidence-coverage disclosures.
  - **Provider calls freeze one coherent runtime and close safely (`backend/utils/llm_client.py`, settings provider, Literature and Painter agents)**: in-flight requests lease their transport/config snapshot across retries, protocol fallback, streaming, deep research, and image polling. Client retirement waits for active calls and remains retryable after close failure; empty/reasoning/tool-call recovery and provider-specific image generation/edit fallbacks no longer mix configuration revisions or lose otherwise valid results.
  - **Production is one prebuilt FastAPI application (`Dockerfile`, `docker-entrypoint.sh`, `start.sh`, `backend/routes/root.py`, frontend entry/hooks)**: a Node build stage produces the Vite SPA, while the runtime image contains no Node toolchain and serves UI, REST, and WebSockets on port 3000. Static assets are opened beneath the configured build root without symlink traversal and streamed from the validated descriptor; API-like and missing extension paths remain real 404s. Native production follows the same single-process topology, while development retains the Vite proxy and hot reload. Lazy panel loading reduces the initial bundle without remounting visited UI state.
  - **The frontend lock now matches the supported toolchain (`frontend/package.json`, `package-lock.json`)**: Vite 8.2.0, Plugin React 6.0.5, React Router 7.18.2, and patched transitive `picomatch` 2.3.2 are installed reproducibly. `npm audit` retains two records for one React Router advisory that applies only to unstable RSC APIs; ScAI is a declarative `BrowserRouter` SPA with no RSC plugin, server actions, or framework server entry, while the patched Router 8 line requires React 19.2.7 and Node 22.22 and removes `react-router-dom`. The audited path is therefore unreachable in this deployment rather than hidden by an incompatible downgrade.

- `0.38.9` — Result figures are now a checked workflow deliverable instead of an optional side effect. Quantitative analyses must produce report-eligible visuals, while integration and genuinely narrative tasks may remain text-only only through an explicit, justified exception. Stage 1 now renders the annotation and exploration baselines the report previously lacked, and the figure, report, download, restore-branch, and frontend paths all agree on which rendered bytes are current. The modified-code regression suite passed 503 backend tests and 267 frontend tests; `tsc --noEmit` and `git diff --check` were clean. +10,144 / −682 across 101 files (2026-08-02):
  - **Structured visual contracts close the no-figure escape (`backend/services/figure_contract.py`, planning and post-execution review)**: plan steps carry typed minimum figure requirements and plan/step provenance; result-bearing work cannot finish until matching current-round, current-lineage, report-eligible renders exist. Revised plans inherit unchanged completed visual steps, legacy snapshots retain narrow compatibility, and integration or pure narrative work can opt out only with an explicit reason. Five successful figure-only turns are free, and one bounded 30-step repair window lets a revised plan close a real visual gap after executor exhaustion.
  - **Stage 1 gains platform-owned annotation baselines (`backend/agents/bioinformatics/stage1_baseline_figures.py`)**: when the analysed object supports them, ScAI renders accepted cell-type annotation UMAP, batch/sample UMAP, cluster UMAP, and marker dotplot panels. Stable baseline names refresh after representation changes without overwriting an analysis-owned figure that chose the same name.
  - **Artifact identity follows verified bytes (`PublicationFigure`, `backend/services/report/artifacts.py`, publication routes and callbacks)**: render, preview, and PDF digests bind reviewed records to physical outputs; image decode/size and PDF structure checks reject corrupt or tiny artifacts, archive recovery restores only the bound render, and real-time review events expose the authoritative post-layout-gate state. The restricted automatic supplementary executor cannot create an unreviewed shortcut—incidental image writes are discarded and quarantined.
  - **Reports preserve every eligible result and disclose incomplete coverage (`backend/services/report/`)**: same-name workspace files cannot impersonate registered renders, report regeneration resets report-owned outputs without erasing archives, fallback reports retain figure provenance, and valid unassigned results receive a deterministic `Additional Analysis Results` home. Missing ordinary visual coverage produces an explicit `partial` report with reasons and unreported-figure details rather than a false completed state.
  - **Restores and the UI use the same current-figure inventory (`backend/services/report/lineage.py`, `frontend/src/components/`)**: branch restores clone inherited figure records under unique IDs and filter reports and downloads by lineage, preventing main/branch leakage and excluding unowned workspace files. The Results gallery merges revision events without reviving stale images, keeps plan-step provenance visible, labels platform baselines neutrally, preserves revision-requested states, and shows partial-report warnings while keeping completed artifacts downloadable.

- `0.38.8` — The saved-session picker identified a session by its title, its date, its status and its round count, and by none of the things the rest of the system uses to address one. Every other surface — the API path, the on-disk session directory, each log line, the `docker exec` that reads a run's artifacts — keys on the UUID minted at creation, and that value was already in the picker's own payload and was the one field it never rendered, so pairing a row in the list against a log or a directory meant loading the session to find out which one it was. +6 / −0 across 1 file (2026-08-01):
  - **The row renders `session.id` under the title (`frontend/src/components/sidebar/SessionBrowserModal.tsx`)**: monospace and smaller than the metadata line beneath it, dimmed and truncating, with the full value on the element's `title` so a narrow modal cannot hide it. Nothing changes on the backend — `list_saved_sessions` in `services/session_store.py` has always returned `id`, and the value is the `uuid.uuid4()` assigned in `routes/sessions.py` when the session is created. The row's click target is unchanged: a click that lands on the UUID still loads the session, because making the text selectable would mean intercepting that click and the whole row is the load affordance.
  - **The `0.38.x` history is backfilled (`docs/HANDOVER.md`)**: `0.38.2` through `0.38.5` and `0.38.7` shipped without an entry, leaving this section documenting `0.38.1` and `0.38.6` alone while the figure-pipeline, statistical-gate, request-timeout and marker-database releases were readable through `git log` and nowhere else. Each entry is written from its own commit message and then checked against that commit's diff, which is where the corrections came from — an attribution to the wrong frontend component, a constant described as the single source of a timeout the same commit also wrote as a literal in seven other places, and a claim about what a broken save had left on disk that the code contradicts. The diffstats follow this section's existing convention of counting code only, excluding documentation and the version bump. The header, stale at `0.38.6`, catches up too: `backend/tests/test_version_consistency.py` pins `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md` and `README.md` against `constants.VERSION` but not this file, which is why it drifted unnoticed.

- `0.38.7` — A run stopped on the marker-database HITL dialog and would not leave it: the retry button re-entered the same failure every time. The upstream fault was real but transient — panglaodb.se accepted the TCP connection, hung the TLS handshake for exactly 60 s and dropped it, twice (11:51:52 and 11:52:59), and was serving normally again by 12:07. What turned a three-minute blip into a blocked analysis was `fetch_and_write`'s own structure: the primary download sat bare inside a pass-wide `try`, so its exception went straight to the outer handler and returned False, and CellMarker — the supplementary source, reachable the whole time at 200, 12 MB, 7.2 s from the same container — was never requested. `MarkerDB.usable()` is an any() over the two tables, so that one table would have satisfied the gate and the dialog would never have opened. +463 / −19 across 6 files (2026-08-01):
  - **Each source downloads under its own guard and the return value reports what is on disk (`scripts/fetch_markers.py`)**: the evidence that the supplementary branch never ran is that no log holds a single `CellMarker fetch failed`, `debug.log` has no `bio-bigdata.hrbmu.edu.cn` connection against two for panglaodb.se, and the markers directory held not even the `LICENSE` the old code wrote between the two downloads. The pass now loops over both sources, records the failure per source and continues, and returns True iff at least one of `EXPECTED_TABLES` passes `is_current_revision` — exactly the condition `usable()` tests; `LICENSE` moves after the loop and is written only when a table survived. Replaying the incident shape: before, the pass attempts `['panglaodb']` and leaves no table; after, it attempts `['panglaodb', 'cellmarker']` and leaves a usable database. Two sources down at once still returns False and still opens the dialog, which is what the gate is for.
  - **`_http_get_bytes` retries, and connect and read stop sharing one budget**: it was single-shot, so one blip on a host that works seconds later cost a run. `_HTTP_ATTEMPTS = 3` with `_HTTP_BACKOFF_SECONDS = (2.0, 8.0)`, and only for faults a repeat could change — `_is_retryable` takes 429 and 5xx and transport errors, while a 403 or 404 is the server declining this exact request and is raised at once rather than spending ten seconds of a waiting user's time to be declined twice more. `_HTTP_TIMEOUT = httpx.Timeout(connect=15.0, read=120.0, write=30.0, pool=10.0)` replaces the flat `timeout=120`: they shared one budget, so a hung handshake burned the allowance that exists for CellMarker's 12 MB body. Against an unroutable primary the pass now fails over in 58 s leaving a usable database, where it previously spent 60 s to produce nothing.
  - **The reason a source failed reaches the dialog, and the frontend had to be opened for it to be visible at all**: `LAST_FETCH_ERRORS` holds `{table: reason}` per pass, cleared at the top so a success never leaves a stale one behind and a False return always carries a current one — including the non-download cases, where a held lock or an outer-handler fault records under the key `marker database`; `marker_priors.last_fetch_errors()` hands back a copy and `_wait_for_marker_db_intervention` in `backend/workflow/core.py` appends it to the prompt as `Last attempt: …`, omitted entirely when there is no reason. That alone would still have shown nothing: the marker branch of `LlmTimeoutDialog.tsx` hid the expandable error detail, which sat inside the info section rendered only when the reason is not `marker_db_missing`, that branch having no model or server to show. A second disclosure now sits next to the description, `whitespace-pre-wrap` so the appended block keeps its line breaks.

- `0.38.6` — Added the ZnT1-Ctr1-DKO demonstration data preparation pipelines, documenting how the platform's example dataset is produced from raw sequencing output. The raw inputs themselves (loom, h5, pathology TIFs, biochemistry images) are distributed outside version control: two of them exceed GitHub's 100 MB per-file ceiling and together they would add ~900 MB to every clone, so the repository carries the scripts that generate the dataset rather than the dataset itself (2026-08-01):
  - **Bulk RNA-seq data preparation pipeline (`demo/ZnT1-Ctr1-DKO/data-prepare/bulk-RNA-Seq/`)**: eight scripts covering the complete workflow from software installation through differential expression analysis. Includes `1.install.sh` for STAR, samtools, and R package dependencies; `2.prepare.sh` for reference genome and annotation preparation; `3.align.sh` for STAR alignment with quality metrics; `4.qc_extended.sh` for post-alignment quality control; `5.correlation.R` for sample correlation analysis; `6.edgr.R` for edgeR differential expression; `7.deseq2.R` for DESeq2 analysis; and `8.annot.R` for gene annotation and result formatting.
  - **snRNA-seq data preparation pipeline (`demo/ZnT1-Ctr1-DKO/data-prepare/snRNA-seq/`)**: three scripts for single-nucleus RNA-seq processing. Includes `1.align.sh` for Cell Ranger alignment; `2.rmsk.sh` for repeat masking analysis; and `3.velocyto.sh` for RNA velocity analysis.
  - **Demonstration inputs stay untracked (`.gitignore`)**: `demo/*/upload/` is ignored, so a local copy of the example data can sit at exactly the path the pipelines and the platform expect without ever being staged. The pathology, sequencing, and biochemistry inputs are obtained separately and dropped into that directory.

- `0.38.5` — A running analysis showed the banner `LLM request retry 1/5 (TimeoutError, waiting 2s)` attributed to the bioinformatics agent, and the attribution was not something the notice had checked: the label was hardcoded, so across 28 surviving sessions on three hosts, 159 of 159 retry notices say "bioinformatics" and 106 of 158 are wrong. Chasing that one banner through the logs of all three deployments surfaced four unrelated defects, none of which the banner was reporting — the notice's hardcoded label, a per-request deadline shorter than a normal completion on a reasoning model, a pathology parser discarding batches the model had answered correctly, and `debug.log` losing its own retention to base64 image payloads. 4,190 passed, 79 skipped, 1 failed — `test_lfc_shrink`, `pydeseq2` absent from the host python, the same inherited failure recorded at `0.38.4`; `tsc --noEmit` clean. +323 / −29 across 17 files (2026-08-01):
  - **A bare JSON array was read as a one-tile object and the whole batch thrown away (`backend/agents/pathology_agent.py`)**: `safe_parse_json` finds the first `{` and balances brackets from there, so a response that is a top-level array comes back as the array's own first element — a one-tile dict with no `tile_findings` key, which `_validate_tile_batch` reports as "tile_findings must be an array" and the caller drops as a schema violation, discarding findings the model had produced in full. Both shapes are unambiguous, so the new `_coerce_tile_batch` accepts either, falling back to `safe_parse_json_array`, which already lived in `backend/utils/formatting.py` and was already called from three other modules. Replaying all 6,580 real tile-batch responses on this host: 1,088 batches newly accepted (16.5% of every batch ever run), 450 still rejected for pre-existing reasons, 0 newly rejected — the change only widens. Of the 1,184 bare arrays 91.9% now validate; the remaining 92 carried six entries for a five-tile batch and still fail on count, which is the model miscounting rather than a parse problem. The loss was smaller than the error count suggests: bare arrays are ~84% of that error string, the rest is unparsable output the coercion deliberately leaves alone, and the single validation retry already rescued roughly half the occurrences. Every bare array came from one model (`Lingjian-1.5`) and the four other models ever assigned to the pathology agent produced none, but all four clean ones are frontier-scale and the only non-frontier model in the corpus is the failing one, so that comparison has n=1 in the cell it would need to rule out model size.
  - **The retry notice's agent label is forwarded rather than assumed (`backend/utils/llm_client_parts/retry.py`, `backend/workflow/core.py`)**: `retry_with_backoff` took `agent_name` but its `on_retry` callback signature did not carry it, so `_install_retry_notifiers` had nothing to label with and passed `AgentType.BIOINFORMATICS` unconditionally — sending anyone reading the notice to the wrong logs during the one event the notice exists to explain. The callback is now five-argument and `_retry_agent` maps the name through `AgentType`; a name with no enum member ships unlabelled rather than being attributed to an agent that did not make the request, which is the honest failure mode but does mean `report_generator` notices carry no label. `TechniqueAgent` never called `_set_llm_context()`, so it inherited whichever agent last touched the shared client and would have been mislabelled even after the fix; it now sets its context in both `lookup_docs` and `lookup_from_error`. The two modules are coupled by positional arity across an unconditional `except Exception: pass`, so a shape drift emits no log line and no notice at all — the two new tests in `backend/tests/test_retry_classification.py` were each checked to fail on a copy of the tree with the forwarding reverted, while the notifier test added alongside them stays green there, which is why both exist.
  - **The per-request deadline is now `DEFAULT_REQUEST_TIMEOUT = 600.0` (`backend/utils/llm_client_parts/retry.py`)**: a reasoning model routinely spends several minutes on one non-streamed response, and abandoning the socket does not cancel the generation — the relay runs it to completion and bills it, so every retry pays for tokens it will never receive. Two bioinformatics timeouts on `gpt-5.6-sol` were HTTP 200 upstream after 6m03s and 5m40s (~10.8k completion tokens) against a 300s client deadline, with the proxy and the relay healthy throughout and serving neighbouring requests in 14-30s. The constant feeds the driver default, the httpx read timeout, both protocol probes and the streaming path; the report vision fallbacks and the pathology overview were raised to the same 600 as literals rather than importing it, and the pathology tile batch takes 600 as a floor under its per-image budget. Consequence worth stating rather than discovering: `_build_retry_kwargs` suppresses retries only *above* the default, so a call passing exactly 600 still gets four, and the worst case for one stalled request therefore moves from 25 to 50 minutes before the HITL gate opens.
  - **The `openai` and `anthropic` logger trees are pinned to INFO, and `httpcore` is deliberately not (`backend/config/_config.py`)**: the two SDKs share a generated base client that logs the entire request body at DEBUG, so one vision call copies its image data URIs to disk verbatim — single records measure 8.0-8.5 MB, `openai._base_client` accounts for 99.86-99.94% of every rotated file on all three hosts, and an image-heavy round rotates the 50 MB file in minutes, leaving roughly a hundred seconds of history, at a third of a second of CPU per record with `SecretRedactionFilter` scanning the rendered megabytes on the request path.
