Both trees have to be pinned because ScAI probes three protocols and routes to Anthropic Messages for some `base_url`s, so pinning one leaves the leak open on the other. `httpcore` logs connection lifecycle only — `connect_tcp`, `start_tls`, the proxy handshake — never a body, at 10,014 bytes of a 49.6 MB file, so silencing it would buy 0.02% and remove the only evidence distinguishing a failed handshake from an application error when running behind `proxy_url`.
  - **What was left unfixed, and one repair that was rejected**: `create_overview_image` still bounds only the longest edge and `image_to_base64` is still PNG with no quality parameter, so nothing caps the encoded byte count of a vision payload — worth fixing on cost grounds, but not for the reason it first appeared to be. The biochemistry context-length 400s it was thought to explain are non-deterministic on byte-identical input and succeed on unchanged retries, and are better explained by channel routing behind a shared model name; making those 400s non-retryable, which looked correct, would have converted eight recoveries into hard failures. Separately, the same model that emits bare arrays also delivers correctly-wrapped responses in the reasoning field rather than content — `_extract_content_from_message` recovers them so no evidence is lost, but the diagnostic path logs "empty content" twice per successful request, which is misleading noise rather than harmless noise.

- `0.38.4` — A kernel whose dill restore had failed needed only a single `import scanpy as sc` to disarm the guard that keeps a broken namespace off disk: dill then wrote an empty pkl over the last good `session.pkl`, and the next resume rehydrated it and reported restore complete. That was one of 23 defects confirmed by executing the shipped code against every change since 0.37.1, then re-confirmed on 0.38.3 — the release that had landed meanwhile touched nine of the same files and closed none of them. They fall into three that could destroy a run's live state, six where the platform refused correct work or accepted wrong work, nine in the figure and report pipeline, and five in durability and provenance. Every fix is demonstrated by running the shipped code against a snapshot of the pre-change tree, and each of the 63 added regression tests was checked to fail on that snapshot; full suite 4180 passed / 79 skipped, the two failures inherited — `test_lfc_shrink` (pydeseq2 absent from the host python) and a stale test in an uncommitted file belonging to another session — both failing identically before any change, and `tsc --noEmit` clean. +2599 / −167 across 39 files (2026-08-01):
  - **The restore-failure guard asks the kernel what it holds, not how many cells it ran (`backend/utils/sandbox.py`)**: `runtime_revision` advances on any successful cell, so requiring it to pass the mark taken when persistence was latched off meant one import disarmed the latch — modules never reach the pkl, so that save dumped a namespace with no analysis state in it over the previous good copy. `save_session` now compares the kernel's live persistable globals against `_restore_failed_globals`, the baseline `_snapshot_persistable_globals` reads while the kernel is still alive at the moment the restore aborts; only names added on top of that residue count as a rebuild, and `None` (the kernel could not answer) is kept distinct from the empty set so an unknown baseline refuses rather than guesses. The revision comparison survives as a cheap short-circuit before the exec lock is taken. Deliberate residual: the probe compares name sets, so a recovery that only rebinds names already in the partial-restore residue defers the save until a new name appears — it fails safe by keeping the previous good pkl, but a fully in-place recovery never persists.
  - **The capture memory preflight under-measured the copy it exists to refuse, and its override outlived the capture it was granted for (`backend/services/snapshot_v2/runtime_parts/kernel_templates.py`, `services/snapshot_v2/failure_gate.py`, `runtime_parts/capture.py`, `backend/routes/lifecycle.py`)**: `_runtime_matrix_bytes` de-duplicated on `id()` while holding no reference to the objects those ids came from, so a `.X` or `.raw.X` that materialises a fresh temporary was freed on return, CPython handed the same address to the next array, and that layer measured zero; `obs`, `var` and `uns` were never measured at all although `copy()` duplicates the frames and deepcopies `uns`. `_seen` now maps id → object, and `_runtime_frame_bytes` (`memory_usage(deep=False)`, which is what a block copy actually costs) plus `_runtime_container_bytes` (reference walk only, depth 8, 20000-node budget) cover the rest. The one-shot "capture anyway" override was force-written to disk the moment Proceed was pressed but consumed only inside a capture's h5ad branch, so a dispatch aborting earlier left the preflight disabled for the whole session; it is now honoured only alongside an in-process grant (`_MEMORY_OVERRIDE_GRANTS`), spent at the top of the attempt rather than at the preflight call, and cleared on terminate, on gate re-entry and on a dispatch that raises. `enter_snapshot_failure_gate` also derived its action set only when `allowed_actions` was absent while `SnapshotFailureRecord` defaults it, so a memory refusal arriving through `pre_restore` never offered Capture anyway and — the preflight being deterministic and `pre_restore` unskippable — Terminate was the user's only exit.
  - **The resampling floor is scoped to the sentence and to the score that sentence names (`backend/services/statistical_gates.py`, `backend/agents/bioinformatics_agent.py`)**: `check_resampling_resolution` decided a whole finding was a resampling result if any part of it matched and then applied the floor to every p-value in the text, so the ordinary shape of a finding — a model-fit result and an enrichment result in one paragraph — got the analytic p-value refused against a floor that does not apply to it. The floor was `min()` over every registered count, letting a 100-draw probe bound a 10,000-draw run when the only rewording that satisfied the gate overstated the uncertainty a hundredfold; `_resamples_for` now follows the score named in the sentence (`_names_score` compares word tokens, so prose naming a panel resolves an identifier like `hallmark_gsea`) and otherwise falls back to the most permissive count, refusing a correct number being the costlier error. It also consults `_CONVENTIONAL_CUTOFFS` as its sibling twenty lines below already did — a 1000-draw default puts the floor within rounding distance of 0.001, which produced a refusal reading "states a p-value of 0.001 … the smallest value is 0.001" at `%.2g`, now rendered `%.3g`. Both call-site filters are gone: `_gate_samples_per_group` keeps groups with `sample_count == 1` (exactly the input `check_analysis_unit` and `check_permutation_at_small_n` exist to refuse, and withholding them emptied the mapping so both gates short-circuited), raising the count only where per-sample-numbered files resolve more; `_gate_score_definitions` carries an earlier round's score forward unless the gates refuse that record on its own, because dropping it took the exemption away together with the check.
  - **A round allocates figures only where exactly one section claims it (`backend/services/report/figure_placement.py`, `backend/agents/supervisor/report_content.py`)**: `enforce_figure_placement` assumed a round has one owning section, but the legacy single-pass outline organises by analysis type and gives four sections the identical rounds tuple, so `setdefault` made the first claimant the owner of everything and relocated every figure into the quality-control chapter while the prompt builder told the other three they had produced nothing. Both sides now build a claimant set and allocate only where it is a singleton; a shared round is left where the model put it and the prompt says so. Placement also deleted any `[FIGURE: name]` not exactly in the map — defeating the HTML renderer's .png↔.jpg fallback, which exists because figures are saved as JPG and the model writes `.png` — and took the model's caption with it, so `_resolve`/`_alt_ext` now rewrite the marker in place and leave a correctly written one byte-identical. And `by_id` was last-wins while `align_sections_to_outline` appends a duplicated id to the extras, aiming every relocated figure at a stray repeated chapter after the conclusions; it is first-wins.
  - **Severity, not artist order or violation kind, decides what the layout gate reports (`backend/services/figure_layout.py`, `backend/agents/bioinformatics/figures.py`, `backend/services/report/artifacts.py`)**: each section caps its report at `_MAX_PER_KIND` = 8 with a head slice over a list collected in artist-insertion order, so fifteen advisory crowding hits on one panel could push every blocking hit out of the report and an unreadable figure shipped badged Approved; `_worst_first` sorts blocking ahead of advisory before the slice. The foreign-axes check exempted only "figure title" from owner −1, so `fig.legend` — the placement the platform's own prompts recommend, whose default location sits over a panel — emitted blocking overlaps and produced a permanently unpublishable figure; figure-level text is now advisory there and the verdict is left to the text-over-text check that compares it against all other text. `format_violations_for_prompt` labelled every collision hit BLOCKING from its kind while discarding the severity it had just read for sorting, instructing the reviewer never to approve what `blocking_violations()` does not hold back. An unverified layout was stored as an empty violation list, indistinguishable from measured-clean, which 0.38.3 made worse by waiving the review-status check for diagnostic artifacts and leaning entirely on the layout gate: a figure whose lint crashed had nothing look at it at all. The renderer now records a `lint_skipped` marker (advisory, since a redraw cannot fix a measurement that never happened) and `is_report_eligible` requires `layout_was_measured` before the diagnostic waiver applies.
  - **The round boundary is stamped when the round opens (`backend/utils/expression_contract.py`, `backend/workflow/agents.py`)**: `round_start_ns` read `state.analysis_log`, but the bioinformatics agent accumulates its steps in a local list and they are merged in — and stamped with `round_number` — only after the round's execution finishes, so on every main pass the current-round lookup found nothing and the function returned `time.time_ns()`, a bound nothing predates. `unchanged_artifact_stems` therefore returned the empty set: the file-stem staleness mechanism was dead on every main pass, 0.38.2's own round scoping was inert with it, and a figure drawn from raw counts then invalidated later in the same round shipped as a current result while `describe_invalidated_artifacts` told the agent that N artifacts had been withheld. `record_round_start` writes `ROUND_START_KEY` into `sandbox_state` before anything that can write a figure runs, idempotent per round so stage 2 keeps stage 1's stamp, and the remaining fallbacks are ordered to include rather than exclude — the last step of an earlier round, else 0.

- `0.38.3` — A completed 8-round run shipped a report whose quality-control chapter was 3,774 characters of fluent prose over the exact numbers a panel would have plotted, with zero figures and zero tables. Two independent defects, one upstream of the other. The figure was never drawn: across all seven real sessions on this host, spanning 0.34.x to 0.37.6, not one of 78 publication figures is a quality-control panel, and in the subject session — where the pass itself ran in full and printed tables — exactly two of 146 sandbox executions contain a plotting call, both `imshow` re-displays of uploaded images, and the 18MB notebook has zero image outputs. Separately, the report collector was losing figures it already had. The platform now renders the panel itself, and `excluded_artifact_stems()` reconciles the two staleness mechanisms that were fighting over what may be shown. Backend suite 4117 passed / 2 failed, both pre-existing and unrelated (`test_citation_integrity`, and a snapshot-v2 test from concurrent work not in this commit); the 0.38.2 baseline was 4060 passed / 2 failed. +1326 / −53 across 22 files (2026-08-01):
  - **The platform draws the exploration round's quality-control panel (`agents/bioinformatics/exploration_diagnostics.py`)**: asking the model is half the fix, and the same class of prompt-only figure rule has already failed here — `services/report/figure_placement.py` exists because "each figure appears exactly once" produced reports embedding one figure three times. `ensure_exploration_diagnostic_figure` runs a cheap probe first, reporting which per-observation metrics the kernel actually holds, because the publication wrapper saves in a `finally` block: a plotting cell that decides mid-way it has nothing to draw still writes an empty canvas, so "nothing plottable" has to be decided before any drawing starts. The column vocabulary is scanpy API names rather than biology, capped at `MAX_DIAGNOSTIC_METRICS = 6` distributions plus a variance profile. `parse_probe_output` rejects a `var` that is not an identifier — it is the one probe value the panel code interpolates unquoted, and the upstream code-safety validator returns clean on a `SyntaxError`.
  - **It renders after both stages of the exploration round, not only the first (`workflow/agents.py`)**: every successful Stage-2 cell that mutates the expression representation calls `invalidate_current_artifacts` for round 1, which would set `is_current=False` on a panel written at the end of Stage 1 and drop it from every surface — that check sits upstream of `diagnostic` in `is_report_eligible`, so the waiver would never be consulted. Session 86a9255a lost its only round-1 figure to exactly that. The figure is upserted by name, so the second render replaces the first and describes the representation the round actually ended on. The plan audit could not have flagged the gap either way: `METHOD_SIGNATURES` holds tool families and no plotting token, so a step promising a plot is `verifiable=False` and by design never counted as unmet — round 1 recorded 38 steps, 0 unmet, 32 unverifiable, and skipped its review as "Plan fully evidenced".
  - **`PublicationFigure.diagnostic` waives the review check and only that check (`models/schema_parts/outputs.py`, `services/report/artifacts.py`)**: such a panel has no reviewer by construction, and `is_report_eligible` gated it on a review status it could never earn. The layout gate still applies, because whether text is covered is measured by the renderer rather than attested by a reviewer, and is therefore the one question an unreviewed figure can still be asked. The field defaults `False`, so sessions written before it deserialize unchanged. The Results gallery now reads `diagnostic` off the record instead of assuming `False` (`workflow/reporting_parts/graphic_abstract.py`): discovery only knows a figure is diagnostic from the directory it sits in, and this panel is written into the scanned workspace, so the per-round coverage floor and the PI rubric were both seeing it as an ordinary result figure. `figure_type` gains `"qc"`, and the PI gallery rubric keeps one quality-control overview rather than excluding every diagnostic.
  - **`excluded_artifact_stems()` in `services/report/artifacts.py` is the single reconciliation point**: the excluded set was the union of non-eligible publication stems and `state.invalidated_artifact_stems`, the second a flat monotonic set of bare names that only exists to reach artifacts with no record at all — a plain `savefig` left in the workspace. Letting it overrule the per-record, round-scoped `invalidate_current_artifacts` inverted that relationship, withholding the reviewed figure while keeping the unreviewed file beside it; 0.37.9 extended the check to the publication tier and turned a latent inconsistency into figure loss. Now a stem does not withhold an artifact whose own record still says it is current, while stems with no record stay excluded — nothing at read time can tell a genuinely stale file from one the pre-0.38.2 producer named by mistake. 0.38.2 bounded the producer with `since_ns`, but the sets already written into `session.json` are unfixable there and every report surface only reads them. Replaying session c31c0b42 through the collector: 0.38.2 yields 5 figures, this yields 10, and the 0.37.6 report it shipped had 10; the five recovered are current, layout-clean and accepted, blacklisted three rounds earlier by a numpy-only `np.log1p` misread as an expression-representation change, and three of them are the exploration round's own.
  - **`_collect_figure_paths` deduplicates by content hash, and `enforce_figure_placement` reports `figureless_sections`**: every dedupe before it compared filename strings, so one file reached under two names embedded twice — three such byte-identical pairs exist in the sessions on this host. The scope is stated in code and pinned by a test: this collapses two names over one file, not one drawing re-encoded twice, and c31c0b42's PNG/JPG pair of a single panel is the latter and still reaches the report twice. Whichever copy carries a publication record wins, so the surviving image keeps its round number, review status and description. `figureless_sections` names outline sections that own a round yet show nothing from it; it is observation only and never gates, because that failure hides perfectly — the chapter above read as complete and nothing anywhere said so.

- `0.38.2` — A completed 7-round run produced 59 images and the Results tab showed 6, none of them from the final round. Replaying the shipped code reproduces the funnel exactly — 59 produced, 21 discovered, 0 surviving the stem blacklist, 9 candidates, 6 selected — and across 27 real sessions the exclusion filter drops 1211 of 1589 candidates (76%), with 24 of the 27 carrying a non-empty blacklist. Four independent defects, one per layer, each fixed where it lives; on session `0e49a6e7` the replay moves discovery 21 → 59, blacklist survivors 0 → 44, and the PI candidate menu 9 → 50. +891 / −89 across 20 files (2026-08-01):
  - **The file-stem blacklist is now bounded by round (`backend/utils/expression_contract.py`)**: `invalidated_artifact_stems` was a monotonic union with no round scope, so one representation-mutating cell in round N blacklisted every artifact from rounds 1..N−1 for the rest of the session, and the only way back out was regenerating that exact figure. Its own sibling `invalidate_current_artifacts` had always been round-scoped (`artifact_round not in (0, round_number)`); the file-stem path never got that rule. `unchanged_artifact_stems` takes a `since_ns` bound — defaulted rather than required because nine `backend.agents.bioinformatics` modules re-export it — and both call sites pass `round_start_ns(state)`, which returns `time.time_ns()` when the current round has logged no step, so a round that has produced nothing blacklists nothing. Invalidation was also silent: all seven consumers read the record as "hide from output" and none as "regenerate", and no prompt or tool result ever mentioned it, so the producer could not repair the deficit it had just caused; `describe_invalidated_artifacts` now names the affected stems (up to `_MAX_NAMED_STALE_ARTIFACTS`, 12) in the tool result the agent reads back.
  - **A module deny-list under the mutation detector (`_NON_EXPRESSION_CALL_ROOTS`)**: `mutates_expression_representation` matched the bare names `normalize_total`, `log1p`, `scale` and `normalize_per_cell` on any module. `np.log1p(<expr>)` was not merely ambiguous but a guaranteed positive — its `BinOp` argument resolves to no target root, which takes the "unknown source, assume canonical" branch — and across 211 real detector firings 68 (32%) were numpy-only calls of that shape. In the subject session the sole round-7 trigger was `cpm = np.log1p((lib / max(total_counts, 1)) * 1e6)`, and it invalidated all four of that round's conclusion figures. Calls rooted at `np`, `numpy`, `pd`, `scipy`, `sklearn`, `torch` and their neighbours are now read as array maths; `sc.pp.log1p(adata)` and a bare `log1p(adata)` still fire, and a call whose module is unknown still counts as canonical.
  - **Discovery reaches wherever the agent chose to save (`backend/services/report/figure_discovery.py`)**: `discover_workspace_figures` scanned three fixed non-recursive globs while nothing constrains where generated code writes a figure, and 38 of the subject run's 59 images sat in a directory of the agent's own invention and never entered the funnel — though the detailed report, which resolves via `rglob`, had been showing them all along. A bounded recursive tier closes it (`_MAX_SCAN_DEPTH = 3`, skipping dot-directories, `__pycache__`, `reports` and `tables`), with the three named roots demoted to a priority ordering that still wins any stem they also hold; `annotation_validation` is reached but tagged `diagnostic` and sorted last. Ordering flips to mtime-descending in the same commit, because callers cap by head-slicing (`DISCOVERY_CAP = 200`) and ascending order made the cap discard exactly the newest figures.
  - **The gallery unions rather than replaces (`frontend/src/components/ResultsPanel.tsx`)**: the `integrated_report` handler overwrote `publicationFigures` with the PI's curated list; the browser already held 16 figures and rendered 6, and the other 10 were unreachable from the UI. Measured over 24 sessions that message narrows the gallery in 14 and widens it in 7, so the curated list is treated as a highlight reel and merged on `figureKey` — name first, id only as fallback, because ids are regenerated for workspace-only figures on every assembly — with curated entries ordered first. An empty figures array is truthy and was used to empty a populated gallery; the guard is now on length. `FigureGallery` previews `PREVIEW_COUNT` (8) and expands on demand, and `ResultsPanel` links the full report, which it never did.
  - **Browsing eligibility is deliberately weaker than publication (`backend/services/report/artifacts.py`)**: `is_gallery_eligible` withholds only artifacts superseded by newer data (`is_current`, no `invalidated_at`), where `is_report_eligible` — still the rule for every archival surface — also withholds a figure that passed only after revision and one the loop gave up on at the revision cap, both of which exist on disk and are the run's own output. Review status and the renderer's measured verdict travel as badges (`layout_blocking`), so "Final" never appears over a figure whose text is covered. `select_brief_figures` had been choosing "key results" from a name and a plot type while `round` and `description` sat unused on the record; it now receives both, a target of 8–15, and a per-round coverage floor enforced in code (`_round_coverage_floor`) rather than trusted to the prompt. `_BRIEF_PREFERRED_TYPES` also gained `violin`, a type `infer_figure_type` emits and the deterministic fallback had been silently dropping.
  - **Recorded as not fixed (`backend/utils/sandbox_parts/kernel.py`)**: `_FIGURES_DIR` is built from `_WORKSPACE_DIR` before that name is assigned, so `_combined_savefig`, `_capturing_show`, `get_seed` and `check_and_fix_nan` have never executed in any session. Reviving them changes where figures land and lets `check_and_fix_nan` mutate analysis data, which needs its own change and its own review; it would not have rescued any of the misplaced figures in this run. `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md` separately catch up with features already in the code — the methodological rule corpus and statistical gates, panel pre-registration and gene-set provenance, the snapshot-failure action sets, the git conventions, and the eligibility split above.

- `0.38.1` — A finished report opened with a bulk RNA-seq heatmap under "Analysis Methodology Overview", and in another session with a round-3 stress/metal-axis panel under "Quality Control". Neither figure had anything to do with the chapter introducing it, and no component had chosen to put it there. The report mixed two ordering principles in one document — the opening chapters organised by *analysis type*, the later ones by *round* — so every figure had a home under both and nothing arbitrated between them. The report model resolved the conflict the only way left to it, by filename semantics, which is how a panel named `..._qc_overview` came to open a report it had no part in. Backend 4040 pass / 79 skip (4003 before, plus 48 new tests); the one failure, `pydeseq2` absent from the host interpreter, is pre-existing on `0.37.8`. +1611 / −57 across 11 files (2026-08-01):
  - **The chapter list is derived from the rounds that ran (`services/report/report_outline.py`)**: the round is at once the unit of time and the unit of argument — one innovation round pursues one research direction — so a single organising axis yields an order that is chronological and argumentative together, and the conflict has nowhere to arise. Method description moves to a Methods chapter that owns no result figures; the four type-organised chapters that were competing for them (`analysis_overview`, `data_qc`, `clustering_annotation`, `differential_expression`) are gone from a multi-round report. A single-pass analysis keeps the older outline unchanged, because with one QC step and one DE step type headings are unambiguous.
  - **Integration rounds are placed by what follows them, not by what they are**: one with further innovation work after it is a *gate* — it explains why the next direction was chosen — so it belongs inline between the directions it connects, capped at `GATE_SECTION_WORD_LIMIT` words with its conclusions labelled provisional, because later rounds may revise them. The final integration round closes the argument and becomes a full chapter. The predicate is computed from `round_results`, not judged by the model. Previously *no* integration round produced a section at all — only `phase == "innovation"` did — so a run with three integration cycles collapsed them into one generic chapter and the two mid-run syntheses were dropped despite being stored in full (three blobs, 21/13/19 KB, pairwise token overlap ~50%: accumulating, not redundant).
  - **A figure belongs to the round that produced it, and the binding is enforced in code (`services/report/figure_attribution.py`, `figure_placement.py`)**: provenance comes from supervision records, then `PublicationFigure.round_number`, then file mtime against round boundaries. The first source is authoritative but sparse — the sandbox emits its `[Auto-saved]` marker only for figures still open when a cell ends, so analysis code that closes its own figures leaves no record. Measured: one session logged 0 of 25 figures across 190 records, another 35 of 59 all from a single round out of five. mtime therefore carries most figures in practice; on both sessions the three sources together resolve 100%. Enforcement is not left to prompt compliance — the prompt already said "each figure appears exactly once" while a shipped report embedded one figure three times, twice back to back. Cross-round references are relocated, repeats dropped, uncited figures appended to their round's section, and section order restored to plan regardless of the order the model emitted. The degraded `_fallback_report` body is exempt from both passes: it has its own fixed sections and was never written against an outline.

**0.37.x — (2026-07-30 onward)**

- `0.37.9` — A run came back with legends stamped over neighbouring panels and the question was whether the supervisor had failed. It had not, in the sense meant: it was configured with vision, it was shown each figure, and its own system prompt asks in as many words for "legends or annotations that are cut off or overlapping". It answered `severity: none` to all four. The component that diagnosed every defect correctly was the PI figure reviewer, and its rejections were converted into acceptances by a four-line early-accept rule. What ships is the deterministic measurement that rule can no longer talk its way past. Backend 4003 pass / 79 skip in both fixed and randomised order (3966 before, plus 37 new tests); the one failure, `pydeseq2` absent from the host interpreter, is pre-existing on `0.37.8`. +1986 / −40 across 12 files (2026-07-31):
  - **The gate that shipped the figures was `curr_score <= prev_score` (`agents/bioinformatics/figures.py`)**: the revision loop's stalled-score shortcut accepted the current version whenever the quality score failed to rise, stamped `ACCEPTED_WITH_RESERVATIONS`, and returned. It fired at least five times in the motivating session — twice on the figures in question, at 6→6 and 7→7 — while the reviewer's `must_fix` for those same rounds read "Panel D legend must be repositioned so it does not overlap or crowd the lower-right heatmaps" and "the stray tiny p-value above the title must be removed". Because the comparison was `<=`, a score that *fell* also counted as stalled: one figure was accepted at 6→5. Three changes. The shortcut is unavailable while the renderer reports a collision — a stalled score is a judgement about diminishing returns, and covered text is not a judgement, so the two must not share an exit. A strict regression now restores the best rendered version from an in-memory snapshot rather than shipping the worse one. And reviewer *approval* no longer settles it either: with budget remaining, an approval accompanied by a measured collision is converted back into a revision request, because the reviewer judges a downscaled JPEG while the renderer reports the artists themselves.
  - **Prose is not a repair instruction, which is why two rounds scored 6→6 (`services/figure_layout.py`)**: the reviewer could say a legend overlapped something; it could not say which artists, by how much, or where, so each redraw was guessing. `LAYOUT_LINT_SRC` is injected into the publication-figure wrapper and runs inside the kernel — the only place a live `Figure` exists — after the draw and before `savefig`, reporting text-over-text, text-over-foreign-axes, tick-label collisions and sub-6pt text as pixel boxes that go verbatim into the redraw prompt and the reviewer's own context. Blocking or advisory is decided per violation by the linter and read from `severity`, not inferred from the kind: the same kind is either, depending on what collided.
  - **The first detector was 41% false-positive on real figures, and hard-gating it would have emptied reports (`services/figure_layout.py`)**: this is the substance of the release. Measured against a 26-figure corpus built from the platform's own idioms — scanpy `umap` / `dotplot` / `matrixplot` / `stacked_violin`, seaborn heatmaps, colorbars, twinx, tables — nine of twenty-two clean figures were reported blocking, and blocking was non-overridable and removed the figure from every report. Five geometric faults, each of which reproduced on a stock plotting call:
    - `ax.axis('off')` leaves `get_visible()` True. scanpy builds legend columns as an `axison=False` container spanning a gridspec cell with the real legend axes nested inside, so the container counted as a panel its own children spilled into, and its never-drawn tick labels counted as text. A stock `rank_genes_groups_dotplot` yielded seven blocking violations.
    - Axes were grouped only when their rectangles coincided (`twinx`), not when one nested inside another. An `axes_grid1` inset colorbar is inside its parent by construction and its tick labels necessarily land on the parent's plotting area. (`Axes.inset_axes` puts the child in `ax.child_axes`, which `fig.get_axes()` never returns, so that spelling cannot exercise this at all — a test written with it passes regardless.)
    - Rotating a crowded categorical axis is what one *does* about it, and is the remedy the tick-collision message itself recommends. A rotated label's axis-aligned box is far wider than its glyph run, so an 8-category bar chart at `rotation=45, ha='right'` was blocked with zero shared ink, and following the advice never cleared the violation. Only the unrotated case is judged now, where the box is the ink.
    - Polar and 3D projections were blocked out of the box: tick-run adjacency is a claim about labels stacked along a straight axis, and on those the labels follow a circle or a diagonal.
    - `legend_loc='on data'` puts cluster names at cluster centroids, which touch whenever two clusters are close. That is data density, not a layout fault, and no redraw reliably fixes it; same-axes annotation crowding advises rather than blocks.
    Two absolute pixel tolerances are also scaled from a 100-dpi baseline, because plotting code sets dpi freely (`plt.figure(dpi=...)`, `sc.settings.set_figure_params`) and the detector's sensitivity must be a property of the layout rather than of a number the agent happened to pass — the module comment had claimed dpi-invariance that two of its constants did not have. Two thresholds moved with it. The out-of-view tick filter tested the box centre, which `ha='right'` and rotation displace off the tick by half the box — and a label the filter drops is invisible to *every* check, hiding real spills; it tests for disjointness now. The tick-run comparison had no fractional guard, so under 1 px of box overlap blocked, while a text box carries ascent/descent leading its glyphs do not fill; it now requires a fraction of the label's own extent. On the same corpus the result is nineteen of twenty-six figures completely silent, and the only clean-figure reports left are two the corpus mislabels: a two-panel `sc.pl.umap` whose first panel's legend is genuinely overprinted by the second panel's ylabel, and a dotplot whose size-legend ticks genuinely render as `80100`.
  - **A correction to what `0.37.9` first claimed about the tick rule**: the one-dimensional projection was justified here as necessary because "an intersection-area rule would let a 36-row column through". Measured, it does not: on the exact figure the test builds, the shipped area predicate flags 35 of 35 consecutive pairs, overlapping by ~270 px² or 54% of the smaller box. The development failure that prompted the change was a `_PAD` applied to both dimensions, shrinking a 4.9 px vertical overlap to 0.9 px — not the choice of predicate. The projection is kept because it is the right *shape* for a one-dimensional run and because the fractional guard is naturally expressed in it, not because area cannot work.
  - **Removed: the out-of-canvas check**. Every save here goes through `bbox_inches='tight'`, which grows the canvas rather than cropping, so being outside the nominal `fig.bbox` costs nothing — while perfectly placed labels exceed it routinely on figures whose axes scanpy sizes independently, and on every 3D projection. It produced only noise.
  - **Measuring must not move what is delivered**: a layout engine re-runs on every draw and is not idempotent, so the linter's own `draw()` shifted the saved image relative to the coordinates it had just reported (8.5% of plotting area under `layout='tight'`). The engine is frozen after the measuring draw, so the figure keeps the layout that draw produced — the one `savefig` would have produced on its own — and the boxes reported are the boxes saved. Verified on matplotlib 3.10 and on the 3.11 the analysis container runs.
  - **Strengthening the supervisor was the obvious repair and is the one thing that cannot work**: three independent reasons, each sufficient. Its prompt already contains the overlap instruction and it still returned clean, so the deficiency is not one of instruction. Its verdict is structurally advisory — `force_skip` is `is_stuck and pi_escalated`, `is_stuck` derives from `_recent_errors`, and that list is appended to only when `code_error` is truthy, so a figure that rendered without raising can never trigger it whatever the verdict says. And the one prompt in the codebase aimed squarely at this defect class, `callbacks.py`'s `_supervisor_figure_review` with its explicit "overlapping text", is unreachable whenever the PI has vision, which is the default. What ships there is calibration, not a gate: the note that sits immediately before the images restates the full checklist rather than a shortened one, since an abbreviated reminder in the highest-recency position quietly narrows the system prompt's criteria; the severity guide stops reserving `critical` for blank figures alone; and `_compress_base64_figure` goes from 512 px to 1024, a 4-5x linear reduction on a 2200 px multi-panel figure being a poor way to ask about tick labels. None of it is relied upon.
  - **One defect was the checklist being followed (`agents/bioinformatics_agent.py`, `agents/bioinformatics/figures.py`)**: the sole legend rule read "place outside plot or use `bbox_to_anchor`". `bbox_to_anchor` moves a legend out of its *axes* and not out of the *figure*, and reserves no space, so obeying it puts the legend on the neighbour's tick labels — which is exactly what happened, and as a regression introduced by a revision, the pre-revision code having had the legend inside the axes at `ncol=2`. The rule now says space must be reserved, by a GridSpec column of its own or `fig.legend`. Alongside it: build with `layout='constrained'` and never combine it with `tight_layout`; annotations outside an axes must set `clip_on` or use `transform=fig.transFigure`, the failure that threw `p<1e-4` onto the suptitle; panel letters anchor with `va='bottom'`, `va='top'` hanging the glyph back into the first tick rows of a short panel; and legend handles are selected by label rather than by slicing `get_legend_handles_labels()` positionally, which is how two series ended up sharing one blue. The same rules are repeated in the revision prompt, because `_revise_figure_code` sends a bare user message and neither `chat()` nor `chat_with_vision()` attaches a system prompt — the agent was redrawing without the constraints it was originally given. Its requirement "Maintain the same overall figure structure" is relaxed to permit re-gridding, since resolving a collision generally requires exactly that, while still forbidding any change to what is plotted. The reviewer's leniency policy keeps waiving colour and spacing preferences after two revisions and stops waiving legibility: a reader cannot recover information from text drawn underneath other text.
  - **`accepted_with_reservations` never meant the figure was readable (`services/report/artifacts.py`, `models/schema_parts/outputs.py`)**: it is reachable both by a reviewer signing off and by the loop giving up, and `is_report_eligible` accepted it either way while nothing downstream read `quality_score`, `pi_feedback` or `must_fix`. Eligibility now also asks whether the renderer measured a blocking collision, and refuses if so. `unresolved_must_fix` is recorded next to it and deliberately does **not** gate — it is a model's opinion rather than a measurement, and hard-gating on it can empty a report of every figure. Also carried forward rather than discarded: a `requires_reanalysis` verdict exits before the revision loop, and used to take the layout half of the same review with it, which is why one figure's duplicate-blue legend was never diagnosed at all.
  - **Four ways the loop could still ship what it had just measured (`agents/bioinformatics/figures.py`)**: the first re-opened the very path this release closes. A revision that runs without raising but creates no figure — `if len(sig): plot() else: print(...)`, the commonest shape in generated analysis code — emits no lint sentinel and leaves the *previous* JPG untouched, so `jpg_path.exists()` and the 2 KB size test both passed on stale bytes and `parse_layout_violations` overwrote a known collision with an empty list, which re-armed both the stall shortcut and report eligibility. An absent verdict now keeps the previous measurement, and the wrapper's own "No figures found" is treated as a failed revision rather than a successful one. A revision that renders blank has already replaced the good file (the wrapper saves in a `finally`), and the loop broke without using the rollback snapshot that exists for exactly that; it restores now. The wrapper also catches a failing `savefig` and only prints, leaving `result.error` None with the previous render still on disk — the same stale-bytes problem from the other direction, and both are now one `_render_landed()` test applied to the revision and the auto-retry paths alike. And the regression rollback fired on any `curr < prev` without checking that the snapshot was better — it is skipped for renders over the size cap, so it can hold something older and worse than what is being discarded. Separately, a figure held back by measured collisions was dropped from every report with nothing on the record saying why; the reason is written into `pi_feedback`, where the figure itself carries it.
  - **Two defects found while reading, unrelated to layout**: `_parse_supervisor_response` exhausts three JSON strategies and then returns a no-op `severity: none` verdict, logging the failure at `debug` — indistinguishable, in every downstream view, from a supervisor that looked and found nothing. All 40 text-mode evaluations in the motivating session took that path. It now logs at `warning` with the response prefix, marks the verdict `parse_failed`, and rejects a non-dict parse instead of returning a bare string — but deliberately does NOT append to `record.technical_issues`, which feeds `steps_with_technical_issues` and prints to the reader as "found N technical issues": a supervisor that could not be parsed is not a defect in the analysis, and counting it as one misinforms the reader about their own results. Separately, `report_html.py`'s publication-figure loop was the one of three collection paths that did not check `excluded_stems`, so nine invalidated stems reached `report.html` through it while the supervision-record loop and the workspace scan filtered them; `services/report/figures.py` was already consistent.
  - **Not done, and why**: re-asserting the house `rcParams` after user code, to undo `sns.set_theme()`. It cannot affect the figure already built — font sizes are resolved into the artists at creation — and every `generate_publication_figure` call re-applies them at the top, so there is no later figure to benefit either. The rendered-font-size check does the job the re-assertion was meant to do, from the artists rather than from the settings. The kernel's global `warnings.filterwarnings('ignore')` is also left alone; the publication path records warnings locally instead and prints matplotlib's layout complaints, which is how "This figure includes Axes that are not compatible with tight_layout" becomes visible without every deprecation notice in the stack following it.

- `0.37.8` — One English verb was silently commissioning analyses nobody planned. The diagnosis held and both halves of the proposed repair were wrong; an independent reviewer, asked to derive its own conclusions rather than check the plan's, refuted them by executing the code instead of reading it, and what ships is neither. Backend 3966 pass / 79 skip in both fixed and randomised order (3963 before, plus three new tests); the one failure, `pydeseq2` absent from the host interpreter, is pre-existing on `0.37.7`. +75 / −3 across 3 files (2026-07-31):
  - **`ingest` is scanpy's label-transfer call and also the verb every plan uses for loading data (`workflow/post_execution_review.py`)**: `METHOD_SIGNATURES` is substring-matched, and `extract_planned_methods` reads it against plan-step prose. So "Ingest the filtered matrix and normalise" — step one of nearly every analysis — read as a commitment to two method families. It was the only token in the 91-entry table that two families claimed, so it charged both at once. The step then audits `unmet`, which flips it out of `PlanAudit.unverifiable` (documented at the property as "never counted as gaps"), triggers the supplementary pass on its own, and renders through `pi/execution_review.py` as "Methods the plan named that nothing ran: integration, reference_mapping" into a PI prompt that is separately told repairing unmet steps outranks everything else. A bounded repair budget was therefore being steered toward batch integration the plan never asked for. `ingest` is dropped from `integration` outright — `sc.tl.ingest` projects labels from a reference, which is `reference_mapping`, not batch correction, so its presence there was a taxonomy error independent of the matching — and qualified to `tl.ingest` / `.ingest(` in `reference_mapping`. Two lines, and the same repair this file already made three entries above, where `regulon` was removed because "regulon itself is prose".
  - **The matcher fix that would have made it worse, and why it was dropped**: the obvious reading is that prose wants whole-token matching while code wants substrings, so the two inputs should be matched differently. The reviewer executed four matchers — the current substring, `_tokens`, the gate side's `has()`, and a punctuation-tolerant word-boundary regex — and all four still return the false positive, because `ingest` is a whole word and no delimiter rule separates the verb from the call. Measured over twenty realistic method strings, the change would have bought three of eight candidate false positives and cost five to eleven true detections: `sc.tl.paga`, `sc.tl.dpt`, `milo.make_nhoods`, `sc.external.pp.harmony_integrate`, `Monocle3`, `CIBERSORTx`, `infercnvpy`. A false negative is the worse failure here — a genuinely dropped trajectory step becomes invisible to the audit permanently, where a false positive costs one spurious PI call. Copying `infer_method_kind`'s `has()` specifically is a trap: it breaks no test while silently dropping all six dotted identifiers, none of which had coverage. They have it now, asserted on both the prose and the code path, so the next attempt fails loudly.
  - **A consistency test between the two method tables would have asserted the opposite of what `0.37.7` pinned (`services/statistical_gates.py`)**: the plan was to enforce that `METHOD_SIGNATURES["composition_model"]` and `_DA` agree, with an allowlist for deliberate divergences. They must not agree. `composition_model` correctly lists `betabinom` and `quasibinomial` because it asks whether a planned step ran; `_DA` correctly excludes them because it asks whether a joint model licenses a composition claim, and `test_statistical_gates.py` pins exactly that. Since `0.37.7` a `_DA` hit downgrades a blocking gate to an advisory, so any pressure to widen `_DA` toward the other table is pressure in the unsafe direction, and the allowlist would have covered half the tokens — a second copy of a comment, not a test. Dropped. What ships instead is a cross-reference in both tables recording that they are different taxonomies over overlapping vocabulary: ten of the seventeen families have no counterpart on the gate side, the one `enrichment` bucket here is the very distinction that side must keep (`gseapy.prerank`, `gseapy.enrichr` and `gseapy.ssgsea` are three inference kinds from one package), and disagreement about members is correct rather than drift. Both say: do not unify them.
  - **A negative fixture, because the table is one edit away from regressing**: ordinary plan prose must commit the plan to nothing. That assertion outlives any particular matcher, which the token fix alone does not. Noted and not acted on: `services/report/pi_review.py` carries a third keyword table with the same `ingest`-under-`integration` error, and is dead — defined, never read.

- `0.37.7` — The two defects `0.37.5` declined as needing a correctness pass rather than a cleanup, now taken. Both live in the statistical gate layer and both are of the same kind: a structured channel that was declared, documented, and never connected. Investigated by five parallel probes and then put to three independent skeptics, one of which refused the first fix — the objection was upheld and the shape of the change altered accordingly, which is the substance of this release. Backend 3963 pass / 79 skip in both fixed and randomised order (3959 before, plus four new tests); the one failure, `pydeseq2` absent from the host interpreter, is pre-existing on `0.37.6`. +110 / −27 across 4 files (2026-07-31):
  - **A gate's escape hatch was unreachable, and connecting it naively would have handed the model an off-switch (`services/statistical_gates.py`)**: `check_composition_claims` opens on a stand-down for a registered differential-abundance method, but `run_gates` was the one caller that dropped a parameter it holds and declares — the five neighbouring gate calls all forward `score_definitions`. Archaeology says it is an accident: the signature arrived as `(findings, *, cluster_labels=(), score_definitions=())` with a docstring saying `cluster_labels` is deliberately not forwarded, the bare call implemented that decision and silently took the second argument with it, and `0.37.5` then deleted `cluster_labels` without revisiting the call site. The obvious repair is to pass the argument, and it is wrong. `method` is free text the model writes — `register_score` declares it as a bare string with no enum — so an early `return []` keyed on it is an off-switch for a `severity="block"` gate, operable by echoing back the four tool names the refusal message itself prints. Worse, the early return precedes the findings loop, so one registered score exempts *every* claim in the payload: a run that registered Milo and then made a per-cluster Fisher test claim about a different cluster went from refused to silently accepted, with nothing recorded anywhere. The check now runs unconditionally and a registered joint model **downgrades** the finding to `severity="advise"` instead of deleting it. The refusal lifts, which was the actual complaint; the simplex caveat still reaches `_escaped_notes` and the report; and the exemption can no longer make a claim disappear. The finding's remedy sentence is chosen to match — telling a model that already registered scCODA to "model the joint composition" is advice it cannot act on. Not comparable to `check_magnitude_claims`, which is sometimes cited as precedent for a payload-wide stand-down: that gate is `severity="advise"` throughout and its hatch reads verified numeric parameters rather than a self-declared name.
  - **`score_kind` read an attribute no producer writes (`services/statistical_gates.py`)**: it consulted `score.method_kind` and fell back to `infer_method_kind`, whose docstring called itself "a migration shim for records written before `method_kind` existed". `ScoreDefinition` has no such field, `register_score`'s schema has no such argument, and nothing in `backend/` ever wrote one — so the declared branch was dead and the shim was, permanently, the only classifier. The branch is deleted, `METHOD_KINDS` goes with it (its sole remaining reference was that branch), and the docstring now says what the function does and why its tables must stay narrow: at `check_declared_testing_family` and `check_ora_background` a name it misses costs a missed check, but at `check_composition_claims` the kind is an *exemption*, so a name it matches too eagerly costs a refusal that should have stood. The alternative — adding `method_kind` to the model and the tool schema so the agent declares its own family — was rejected for the same reason as the `return []` above: it routes a gate's decision through an unvalidated self-report by the party the gate constrains.
  - **The two escape hatches disagreed about names this codebase already blesses (`services/statistical_gates.py`)**: with the structured hatch reachable, `_DA` became load-bearing and three entries were unreachable for the spelling that actually arrives. Matching is whole-token over a code string, so `miloR::testNhoods` does not contain `milo`; `milor` is added, since the prose hatch has accepted `milo(?:py|r)?` all along. `scanpro` is added to both hatches — the prose one already blesses `propeller`, and `post_execution_review.METHOD_SIGNATURES` lists the two side by side as composition models. Four candidates were rejected and the reasons recorded in the source: `quasibinomial` and `glmmTMB` fit one population at a time and so are the design this gate refuses rather than an escape from it; `pertpy` is a broad perturbation toolkit whose composition entry points already match through `sccoda` and `milo`, while the bare name would capture its enrichment and perturbation tools too; and `scipy.stats.betabinom` is a univariate distribution carrying no joint model, which fails the same test as the first two. One hole is knowingly left: `dirichlet`, present since before this release, likewise matches `scipy.stats.dirichlet`. No word list can close that, which is a further argument for the downgrade — a false match now costs a lifted refusal, not a lost warning.
  - **Two documents pointed at the deleted field (`data/method_rules.yaml`, `docs/HANDOVER.md`)**: the ORA rule's `why` — text delivered verbatim to the model on every background refusal — said the check "reads the declared `method_kind` and the declared background". Both halves were wrong: the field never existed, and what makes a correctly backgrounded call pass is `check_ora_background` reading `background_source`, not the classification. It now states the two judgements separately and keeps the accurate historical note that the earlier version matched substrings, which is what made `"ora"` fire inside `"temporary"`. The corresponding `0.37.6` handover line is corrected the same way.

- `0.37.6` — Tier A of a fix set derived from a forensic audit of one completed analysis round, which graded a named mechanism "plausible but not proven". The verdict's direction was right and no framework change could have made the data settle it, but its *confidence* was degraded by defects that had nothing to do with the biology: the headline q-value was a permutation floor shared verbatim by eight panels and read as if it ordered them, two panels sharing 13 of their 17 members were reported side by side as converging evidence, the bounded repair pass spent four of its five iterations on identifiers the session does not hold, and a cell that failed on a one-argument mistake was replayed into the next round under a "do NOT re-run completed steps" banner. Two root causes underneath, and the eleven findings are downstream of them: the gate layer has no numeric channel and therefore reads prose, and every inter-stage interconnect is prose. Each change below puts one structured value where prose was, or acts on metadata the platform already records meticulously and then gates nothing on. Enforcement was chosen per change rather than uniformly — a rejection in this system costs a retry and a step from a 200-step budget, so disclosure and clamping are preferred wherever a refusal could overrule a legitimate design. Backend 3959 pass / 79 skip, frontend 252 pass with `tsc` clean; the one backend failure (`pydeseq2` absent from the host interpreter) is pre-existing on `0.37.5`. +1077 / −24 across 16 files, two new corpus rules, both domain-neutral and enforced as such by the existing raw-source guards (2026-07-31):
  - **A resampling p-value cannot be smaller than 1/(B+1) (`services/statistical_gates.py`, `data/method_rules.yaml`, `agents/bioinformatics/tool_schemas.py`)**: `check_resampling_resolution` refuses a floor value quoted as an estimate, and advises in two weaker cases — no resample count on record for a ranked-enrichment score, and one value shared across three or more results. The refusal is narrow by construction: it fires only when the model itself declared how many resamples it drew and then quoted a value at or under the floor those draws imply, as an equality rather than a bound. That is a self-contradiction in its own record, not a judgement about the analysis, and the fix is a rewording it can perform without recomputing anything. Reporting the same value as an inequality is correct reporting and passes. A 2% tolerance on the comparison catches the floor quoted rounded — 1/501 is written "0.002" — and cannot reach the next attainable value up, which is roughly double. The absent-count case is left advisory because `score_kind` is inferred by matching tokens in a free-text `method` string, and a mis-tokenised method must not cost a rejection. New gate rule `resampling-p-has-a-resolution-floor`; the result vocabulary (`n_resamples`, and its aliases) rides in the existing `ScoreDefinition.parameters` dict, so this is documentation on a schema that already accepts it, not a migration.
  - **Two panels that share most of their members are one test reported twice (`services/statistical_gates.py`, `services/analysis_plan.py`, `data/method_rules.yaml`)**: `panel_overlap` returns shared members, containment and Jaccard for two frozen panels, and `check_panel_redundancy` discloses pairs above a containment majority. Containment rather than Jaccard is the load-bearing choice: a small panel wholly inside a large one is entirely redundant with it while scoring low on Jaccard, precisely because the large panel has other members — the case that motivated the check scores 0.76 containment and 0.48 Jaccard. Advisory, never blocking, and that is not timidity: partitions produced by this platform's own `resolve_partitioned_gene_set` are subsets of one another by construction, ontology parents and children share most of their members by definition, and testing a broad process alongside a focused component of it is ordinary practice. Refusing would be the platform overruling a design decision it is not entitled to make. What it *is* entitled to do is refuse to let the overlap go unstated, which is what turns one observation into an apparent agreement between two. New advisory rule `overlapping-sets-are-not-independent-evidence`.
  - **An evidence grade may not exceed the reproducibility of its sources (`agents/bioinformatics/mechanism_validation.py`, `agents/bioinformatics_agent.py`)**: `validate_mechanism_program_payload` takes an optional `score_provenance` map; a program whose every *resolvable* supporting score was constructed for this analysis is clamped to `moderate`, with the reason and the named sources appended to `missing_evidence`. A clamp rather than a refusal, deliberately: the handler already fabricates a `bespoke:` provenance string after four rejections, so harder pressure on provenance produces more fabricated provenance, not less. Unresolvable citations are ignored — a bookkeeping gap is not evidence about reproducibility — and one reproducible source among several lifts the cap, because a panel built for a mechanism no public resource covers may be the only honest instrument available. The kwarg is keyword-only with a default so the pure-function boundary the eleven facade re-exports depend on is unchanged, and callers that pass nothing keep their behaviour exactly.
  - **Panel size is a property of the panel, not of the excerpt reported alongside it (`agents/bioinformatics_agent.py`)**: `register_score` computed `n_genes` and `panel_hash` from `gene_list`, which the tool schema documents as an excerpt. For any panel above that cap, `n_genes`, `match_rate`, the `underpowered` flag and the drift hash were all wrong — and the underpowered flag is the one structured signal in the system that speaks to whether a negative result means anything. Both now come from `plan.panel_genes`, the frozen canonicalised membership, when a plan is cited; `gene_list` keeps its actual job, which is detecting drift away from that record. The raw-source guard that pinned the old expression was retargeted to the contract instead: power is assessed on the frozen panel, and drift still compares the frozen panel against what was reported.
  - **The PI was asked to name things and shown none of their names (`agents/pi/execution_review.py`, `workflow/post_execution.py`, `agents/bioinformatics/supplementary.py`)**: `review_round_execution` demands tasks specific enough for a code generator to execute unmodified, and was given project prose, an audit summary and findings text to write them from — no registered score names, no `obs` columns, no layers, no artifact names, although all of them sit in `AnalysisState` or one kernel round-trip away. It now takes `session_names` and renders them under a heading that says what the list is, with the instruction split the way it has to be: use an identifier verbatim when you mean something that exists, and say so explicitly when you are commissioning something new. The pattern is copied from `pi/annotation.py`, which has done exactly this for the annotation-validation round since it was written. Separately, the executor's own copy of the object metadata was `json.dumps(metadata)[:2000]` — a byte slice of a JSON document, which on any realistic `obs` is exhausted inside the per-column value summaries and hands the executor syntactically invalid JSON with `layers`, `n_obs`, `n_vars` and `has_raw` silently dropped. It renders field by field now, truncating the lists rather than the string, longest field last.
  - **A failed call and an environment fact are different evidence (`workflow/context.py`, `utils/error_parsing.py`, `workflow/post_execution_review.py`)**: `_build_code_history_summary` never rendered `rec.error`, so a cell that died on a species-cased resource argument reached the next round indistinguishable from a completed one, under a header instructing the agent not to re-run completed steps — which is how a one-argument mistake hardened into a settled limitation across rounds. Failed cells are now labelled and carry their error. `TECHNICAL_ERROR_TYPES` contained no network or transport exception, so a failed remote call was classified as nothing at all: invisible simultaneously to the documentation-lookup trigger, the swallowed-error detector and the availability scan; fifteen names were added. `unavailable_method_families` matched its family tokens against the error text *plus the entire cell source*, so one `ImportError` in a cell that imports several packages before doing anything retired every family named anywhere in it — permanently, un-round-filtered, with the PI instructed never to propose a method the audit lists as unavailable. It now extracts the module the failure actually named, and falls back to the cell's import lines only when the error names none, because "cannot import name 'x'" need not carry its module. The deliberate non-round-filtering is kept: a package missing in round 5 was missing in round 2.
  - **Disclosure of what was not checked, and of what was computed but not reported (`workflow/synthesis.py`, `agents/bioinformatics/supplementary.py`)**: the PI writing a round summary saw `n_steps`, `n_completed`, `n_skipped` and `n_unmet` and no indication that the remainder was never checked — counts that do not sum to the total, presented as a coverage report. `n_unverifiable` is now in the whitelist. And a supplementary pass that ends on its iteration limit returned a hardcoded empty findings list while the columns it had already written sat on the live object: the warning named which *signature keys* had moved (`obs_columns`, `obs_hash`) rather than which columns had appeared, and the next pass then re-requested them under a guessed name. The added column names are diffed from signatures both already in hand, reported in the warning, and carried into the iteration-limit envelope alongside the execution ids that produced them.
  - **The figure budget counted attempts and reported figures (`agents/bioinformatics_agent.py`)**: `has_figure_work` was set before any validation, so a code-validation failure, an execution error, a blank figure under 2 KB, and a refusal issued by the budget itself each consumed a slot — a refused call pushing the counter further past its own limit. The refusal text asserted "(N figures already generated)", which could simply be false. Slots are charged only when the publication-figure list actually grows, accepted figures are counted separately from figure-only iterations, and the message states both.
  - **The planner was never told what this session cannot obtain (`agents/bioinformatics/planning.py`)**: no tool in the twelve-entry surface can acquire an external dataset, and nothing said so, so approved plan steps specified downloading public perturbation data. A step written that way is not skipped when it cannot run — it is silently satisfied by whatever the executor can improvise, and the substitution then carries the weight of the real thing. The planner prompt now states the boundary and asks for such steps to be recorded as out of scope rather than restated as something that looks runnable.
- `0.37.5` — A quality review of everything after `0.36.1` — 24 commits, 270 files, 33.6k inserted lines — run as 21 independent reviewers over five code groups and four lenses: reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude. Not a correctness pass; `0.37.4` was that. The 170 findings reduced to roughly a hundred distinct defects, of which 62 were fixed and the rest declined. The bar for fixing was that the change be observably inert: anything that would have moved a returned value, an emitted payload, a prompt string, a gate threshold or a durability barrier was declined rather than argued with, and the result reproduces the pre-change baseline exactly — backend 3931 pass / 79 skip in both fixed and randomised order, frontend 252 pass with `tsc` clean. (The one backend failure, `pydeseq2` absent from the host interpreter, is pre-existing on `0.37.4`.) Net −68 lines across 63 files (2026-07-30):
  - **Snapshot V2 measured its own storage in two places (`services/snapshot_v2/object_store.py`, `importer.py`, `service_parts/capture_support.py`, `recovery.py`, `exporter.py`, `checkpoint_paths.py`)**: the import admission gate and the capture quota gate each carried a private `_physical_object_store_bytes` and a private walk-up-then-`shutil.disk_usage` probe, and the two size readings had already drifted (`except OSError` against `except FileNotFoundError`). Both now call `physical_object_store_bytes()` and `free_bytes_at()`; the capture-side method survives as a one-line delegate because a test monkeypatches it by name. The third probe, in `runtime_parts/capture.py`, was deliberately left — it carries an extra no-existing-ancestor branch and a different details payload the shared helper cannot reproduce without changing what the gate reports. Three more rules had two authors each: the manifest root-hash derivation (now `compute_manifest_root_hash()`, read by the exporter, `capture_support._compute_root_hash` and `ManifestStore.write_staging`), the stranded staging-temp reclaim `0.37.4` had just added to two modules held in sync by a comment (now `reclaim_stale_staging_temp()`), and the `-wal`/`-shm` companion pair hardcoded at three fresh sites (now `checkpoint_paths.SQLITE_COMPANION_SUFFIXES`). The raw-source guard pinning those two literals inside `RestoreCoordinator.apply` was retargeted to assert the constant is referenced there and that the constant holds those values — same guarantee, one owner.
  - **The staged-copy protocol was written twice, and so was its leak fix (`backend/utils/runtime_apply.py`)**: `atomic_replace_pkl` and `atomic_replace_h5ad` held byte-identical copies of an 18-line temp-name / `copy2` / fsync / `os.replace` / fsync-parent sequence, so the unlink-on-failure fix shipped earlier in this range had to be written, and reviewed, twice. It is one `_staged_copy_replace()` now. Both functions also hashed the file they had just written with `hashlib.sha256(path.read_bytes())`, pulling a multi-GB h5ad through the backend process to produce 32 bytes; both use the chunked `object_store._hash_file`. In the same vein `marker_priors.parse_table_header` slurped a multi-MB TSV and `splitlines()`'d it to read one header line, and `MarkerDB._load` called it two to three times per table — it `readline()`s now, once per table.
  - **The restore safe-point handshake had no implementation, only seven copies of one (`backend/workflow/`)**: every participant re-derived the hook as `getattr(self, "await_restore_safe_point", None)` plus an `inspect.isawaitable` dance, so each new HITL gate had to remember an incantation nothing enforced. All seven call `helpers.ack_restore_safe_point()`. The `WorkflowSupersededError` unwind had the same shape: a helper with the identical body was extracted in this same range and then not used by the four `except` blocks around it, so the shared tail is now `_superseded_state()`, reached from five sites including the extracted helper itself. `wait_with_skip_time` kept its entire pre-change body as a fast path that the polling loop below already subsumed; the fast path is gone and the poll interval is conditional instead.
  - **Agent-side copies of one policy (`agents/bioinformatics_agent.py`, `agents/bioinformatics/`, `workflow/post_execution.py`)**: the `fetch_gene_set` handler bound a fetched panel into the kernel with the same sanitize/execute/fallback block in both its partition and single-set branches — now `_bind_gene_set()`. `_supplementary_code_is_safe` was a character-for-character copy of `_validation_code_is_safe` differing only in which class attribute holds the denylist, with both mixins composed into the same agent — now `shared.code_is_statically_unsafe()`. The post-execution review repeated a four-statement decline-and-record epilogue at every early return, stored its supplementary timeouts three times as pre-multiplied literals, and deduped collected suggestions with a linear scan run twice per round. Two raw-source guards that sliced the tool-dispatch window and asserted on text which had moved into a helper were retargeted the same way as the checkpoint guard: assert the window calls the helper, then assert the helper's own source carries the pinned text.
  - **Export and import each grew a second copy of themselves (`routes/export_import_parts/`)**: `export_state` and `export_prepare` still carried duplicate pre-pack preambles after an earlier commit deduplicated only the tar-building — now `_pack_session_archive()`. The import-job record was a hand-rolled dict literal in ten places across two modules, a shape that had already caused field drift within this range — now `import_job_record()`. `_add_session_logs_to_tar` re-implemented the directory walk and symlink-containment guard of `_add_tree_to_tar` added 140 lines above it in the same commit; the walk is `_contained_files()`. The rewritten `llm.jsonl` was held in memory three times over (a `BytesIO`, its `getvalue()`, and a second `BytesIO` around that) and is held once; zip members extract through `shutil.copyfileobj` rather than `write_bytes(zf.read(name))`; and the redundant first path-remap pass over the whole session document, whose successor covers a strict superset of the same text, is gone. `PREPARED_EXPORT_TTL`, defined in `backend/config` and again in the routes layer where nothing read it, has one definition.
  - **Facts with several authors now have one (`backend/config/`, `backend/state.py`, `routes/interaction.py`, `services/`)**: export temp-file names had four independent producers and a fifth site — the orphan sweep in `tasks/session_cleanup.py` — hardcoding the prefixes it had to match, an unenforceable contract that silently strands full-size session copies when it breaks; all five read `PREPARED_EXPORT_PREFIX` / `EXPORT_STREAM_PREFIX` / `SNAPSHOT_EXPORT_PREFIX` / `EXPORT_TEMP_PREFIXES` from `backend/config` now. "Is anything executing for this session?" was answered by two probes over the same three task registries, in `SnapshotService._has_live_run` and `RestoreJobManager._acknowledge_if_quiescent`; the shared part is `state.has_live_session_task()`, with the service keeping its deliberate fail-open wrapper. `feature_gate.has_unfinished_restore_journal`, added in `0.37.4` and documented as mirroring the startup scan in `main.py`, is now the implementation `main.py` calls. `marker_priors.normalize_species` delegates to `gene_set_resolver.normalize_organism` instead of holding a second alias table, and `report/score_section.provenance_label` renders through `GeneSetProvenance.citation()` instead of re-spelling its four formats. The snapshot-blocked 409, hand-written into four handlers of `routes/interaction.py` with wording already drifting between copies, is `_refuse_if_snapshot_blocked()`; the three `routes/lifecycle.py` sites were left alone because two of them return `APIResponse(success=False)` rather than raising, and one helper cannot serve both without changing a response shape.
  - **Frontend vocabularies were spelled by hand, which is how `0.37.3` lost an action (`frontend/src/`)**: `validSnapshotFailureActions` had silently dropped that release's new `proceed` value with no error and no type failure, and the release fixed it by editing three hand-maintained lists. `SnapshotFailureAction` and `TaskStatus` are now derived from `SNAPSHOT_FAILURE_ACTIONS` and `TASK_STATUSES` const tuples that the runtime validators test membership in, so the class of bug is gone rather than the instance. The `GET /session/{id}/load` → `transformLoadResponse` → `updateSession` chain, open-coded four times, is `reloadSessionState()`. `continueRound` and `resumeAnalysis` were line-for-line duplicates, so the resume-refusal guard added in this range had to be written into both; one is an alias of the other now. `SnapshotTree` re-parsed every timestamp inside its sort comparator on every render and now decorates once inside a `useMemo`, and the `SnapshotFailureDialog` hint gated on a hardcoded `snapshot_memory_insufficient` code reads the server's `allowed_actions` instead.
  - **Dead code, and one domain-specific default (`services/statistical_gates.py`, `workflow/post_execution_review.py`, `services/gene_set_fetch.py`)**: removed `population_vocabulary`, `_LABEL_STOPWORDS` and `_names_population`, whose only consumer documents that it deliberately does not use them; `StepAudit.as_dict` and `PlanAudit.as_dict`, never called; the orphaned `RuntimeDependencyEntry` type; and an unused `known_hashes` parameter whose one write was dead. The `check_cell_level_inference` clause filter, a four-branch chain with three `pass` bodies evaluating one boolean, is a named predicate. Separately, `gene_set_fetch` hardcoded `"mouse"` as the organism a non-native gene set is projected *from*, inside the generic resolver and with an accompanying comment claiming a caller override that did not exist — a domain-specific default in a platform whose defaults must be domain-neutral. It is `DEFAULT_PROJECTION_SOURCE` and a caller-settable parameter now, with the default value unchanged.
  - **Declined, and why**: roughly forty findings were not applied. Most would have changed behavior — unifying the three import materialize paths (which genuinely remap a different number of times each), merging the two tool-name→method-family lexicons (which have drifted, so unifying them reclassifies several tools), moving `_fsync_objects_dir` from per-object to per-transaction, changing when the safe-point hold is released in `jobs.py`, and collapsing the two frontend snapshot poll loops (which have different caps and terminal branches). The rest need a deeper change than a cleanup pass should make: `runtime_parts/kernel_templates.py` hand-maintains a ~200-line transliteration of `sanitize_anndata_for_h5ad` because a kernel source string cannot import host modules, and this release again had to apply one fix to both copies. Two findings look like defects rather than untidiness and are left for a correctness pass: `statistical_gates.score_kind()` reads a `method_kind` attribute no producer writes, so its migration shim is inert, and `run_gates` holds the structured arguments `check_composition_claims` accepts but calls it with neither.

- `0.37.4` — A multi-agent review of everything after `0.36.1` (21 commits, 245 files) returned fifteen findings; each was then re-derived from source by an independent verifier told to refute it. Eleven survived. Four did not, and those four are the useful part: the reported harm was unreachable in every case, but a narrower real defect sat underneath each one, and two severities moved in opposite directions from what the review claimed. A second pass over the resulting diff then found a defect in one of the fixes itself. What ships is the verified set, plus the corrections (2026-07-30):
  - **A gate-parked session could assert it had no kernel while holding one (`backend/services/snapshot_v2/jobs.py`)**: `_acknowledge_if_quiescent` wrote a hardcoded `"kernel_present": False` into the safe-point descriptor. Idle is not kernel-less — the WebSocket runner pops `_run_tasks` before `workflow.close()` stops the kernel, so for the length of a full dill dump the session owns a live sandbox, no task, and a `waiting` status. `_resolve_kernel_present` reads the descriptor before the sandbox it was handed, so `RuntimeCapture.capture` took the no-introspection branch and the pre_restore safety snapshot froze a manifest claiming *complete, nothing to save* over a namespace full of state — surfacing later as a rollback that quarantines the live runtime. The field is now computed from the workflow's own sandbox, so descriptor and sandbox agree. Consequence accepted deliberately: a live kernel with an empty required set is fail-closed by existing policy (`empty is only valid when kernel_present=False`), so a session parked with a started-but-empty kernel now blocks its pre_restore capture instead of silently committing empty-complete. The first attempt refused acknowledgement outright whenever a sandbox was present; that reinstated the very timeout the descriptor exists to prevent and failed `test_parked_session_with_a_live_workflow_object_is_quiescent`, so acknowledgement stays and only the claim changed.
  - **Turning Snapshot V2 off cleared the marker that says a restore is half-applied (`backend/services/snapshot_v2/feature_gate.py`)**: `normalize_disabled_snapshot_state` dropped `recovery_blocked` unconditionally, and gate-off is the default rather than an exotic mode — `SCAI_ENABLE_SNAPSHOT_V2` must be opted into. A restore that crashed mid-`apply()` leaves session.json rewritten and notebook, checkpoint and workspace not; clearing the flag with no recovery having run lets Start drive the graph against four files describing different points in the same run. The unblocking is deliberate and its reason still holds — with V2 off nothing else can ever clear the flag — so it is kept and bounded instead of reverted: `_has_unfinished_restore_journal()` mirrors the startup scan in `main.py`, and a session whose journals still hold a STAGED/STAGING/APPLYING entry stays refused until the gate is turned back on and recovery can finish or roll back. A session merely carrying a stale flag, or one imported from an archive with no journals, still unblocks. Unreadable counts as unfinished; unlike the `main.py` scan, an `OSError` while scanning also fails closed.
  - **Object-store temporaries had no owner after a hard kill (`backend/services/snapshot_v2/object_store.py`, `recovery.py`)**: `ObjectStore.put_*` stages a multi-GB copy at `staging/.{sha}.tmpXXXX`, and its `finally` covers every in-process path — but not SIGKILL, an OOM kill, or a container stop.
