  - **GC protects the audit bundle it was told to retain (`backend/services/snapshot_v2/gc.py`)**: the `audit-` prefix is a listing rule, but `mark()` inherited it as a reachability rule. On the `discard_current` restore path no pre_restore snapshot is taken, so the audit manifest is the only record of the state the user discarded — and the next sweep unlinked every one of its objects, leaving a manifest of dangling refs that no API can delete.
  - **The discard-current escape hatch is reachable *and* can succeed (`backend/services/snapshot_v2/service.py`, `pre_restore.py`, `frontend/src/components/SnapshotPanel.tsx`)**: the client has always branched on `PRE_RESTORE_UNAVAILABLE` to offer "discard my current state and restore anyway", and no producer ever emitted it — so that branch, `DiscardCurrentDialog` and the whole audit-bundle path behind it were dead code, and a user whose kernel could not be captured could only Retry into the same failure. Every pre-restore capture failure now reports that code (cause preserved in details), and the panel acts on it when it arrives on the transaction rather than only on the initial 202. Making the hatch reachable exposes that it could not work: `AuditBundle.capture_locked` handed the live sandbox to `_capture_locked_for_restore` with no `runtime` on the request, so `_capture_impl` re-drove the identical live-kernel capture whose failure produced the code in the first place. Every shared failure cause reproduced exactly, the bundle raised `AUDIT_CAPTURE_FAILED`, and `restore_snapshot` returned `AUDIT_FAILED` — a code the panel offers no action for. The capture is now factored into `AuditBundle._capture` and the kernel captured opportunistically: the live attempt stays first, since while it succeeds it is the only record of the discarded kernel, and on failure the bundle retries once with `sandbox=None` and an explicit `empty_complete_runtime()` — explicit because the empty-runtime eligibility gate refuses `reason="audit_bundle"` and would merely trade one terminal code for another. Non-kernel failures (quota, workspace, state) recur on the retry and still raise, so a bundle is never published on a guess; one published without the kernel records `AUDIT_RUNTIME_NOT_CAPTURED` carrying the original code.
  - **A live-kernel capture cannot publish an empty "complete" runtime (`backend/services/snapshot_v2/runtime_parts/capture.py`)**: the known-unpersistable pre-skip dropped a value that IS live in kernel globals without recording it in `dropped_present`, so the post-pruning fail-closed guard could not fire. The absent-name soft skip is deliberately left alone.
  - **The fail-closed runtime tier is reachable (`backend/services/snapshot_v2/runtime_parts/capture.py`, `service_parts/capture_steps.py`)**: `checkpoint_dependencies` has no production writer, so `mandatory_names` was always empty and the residual tier was "best effort, silently drop". `_collect_checkpoint_dependencies` now also reads the safe-point descriptor (the channel a boundary producer actually has, and the one `_resolve_kernel_present` already uses), and a canonical artifact that is LIVE in globals and that dill unexpectedly failed on now fails closed. Absent names and inherently unpersistable types keep their deliberate soft skip.
  - **A capture never spans two kernel generations (`backend/utils/sandbox.py`, `sandbox_parts/kernel.py`, `services/snapshot_v2/runtime_parts/capture.py`)**: new monotonic `kernel_epoch`, bumped by start and restart and asserted around every capture cell. `_execute_unlocked`'s pre-execute liveness guard restarts a dead kernel and then runs the caller's code on the new one, which `_restart_kernel` rehydrates from the last saved pkl — an older generation than the one the capture already partially serialized. A kernel reaped between two cells produced a manifest whose variables never coexisted, and every object hash in it is internally consistent, so nothing downstream could detect it.
  - **The capture templates unbind their own temporaries (`backend/services/snapshot_v2/runtime_parts/kernel_templates.py`)**: the templates run at the kernel's `__main__` scope, so every name they bind is a kernel global for the life of the kernel, and neither unbound anything. `_SAVE_ANNDATA_TEMPLATE` left `_to_write` — a full sanitized copy of the captured AnnData — plus seven references into it; because `_adata.copy()` is evaluated before the rebind, every later capture peaked at 3x the dataset where sanitize-then-write needs 2x. The waste was invisible: `save_session` skips `_`-prefixed names so it was never persisted, no probe measures kernel RSS, and only a kernel restart reclaimed it. `_SAVE_DILL_TEMPLATE` left `_verified_obj` the same way. Both now end in a `finally` that pops their own temporaries — a `finally` because a failed write is exactly when the host retries and builds another copy, with IPython keeping the failing frames alive on top of that. The cleanup uses only tuple literals so the stray-replacement-field guard on these `.format()` strings still holds, and sits after the markers the host parses.
  - **The live pkl wins over a stale h5ad sidecar (`backend/utils/sandbox.py`)**: the sidecar set is written only by restore/recovery and never retired, while `save_session()` dumps every live AnnData into the pkl. The restore template applied the dill frames and then rebound unconditionally from the sidecars, so every later rehydrate (kernel OOM, session reopen) rolled the namespace back to the restored snapshot — hashes still matched, so nothing flagged it.
  - **`atomic_replace_*` cannot leak a dataset-sized temp (`backend/utils/runtime_apply.py`)**: both functions copied into `<dst>.<pid>.staged` with no guard, so an exception between `copy2` and `os.replace` — ENOSPC being realistic, since restore holds the object-store copy, the staged copy and the live copy at once — left a dataset-sized partial in `<session>/states/`, a directory no sweeper covers: the states sweeps glob `*.h5ad`, GC walks `objects/`, and the snapshot staging sweeps operate on `<session>/staging`. It is not attributed to the session's quota and is packed verbatim into every v1 export. Both now unlink on `BaseException` before re-raising. Since the temp name is keyed on the pid that covers the in-process case completely, but a killed process cannot clean up after itself — so `_quarantine_unreferenced_live_h5ads` additionally reclaims `*.<pid>.staged` entries whose writing process is gone. Liveness, not the name, decides: a temp belonging to a running pid is an in-flight copy and unlinking it would sabotage a live restore.
  - **The post-publish reconciliation flag has a consumer (`backend/services/snapshot_v2/service.py`)**: `_capture_impl` deliberately does not raise when the session save after a successful publish fails, signalling `session_reconciliation_pending` instead — and nothing read it, so the escape hatch had no other end and the `saved_runtime_revision` written just before was simply lost.
  - **Snapshot-failure actions are durable and reachable, and every forward-motion endpoint refuses an armed gate (`backend/routes/lifecycle.py`, `backend/routes/interaction.py`, `backend/services/snapshot_v2/failure_gate.py`, `backend/services/snapshot_v2/errors.py`)**: Skip now closes the held HITL WebSocket the way Retry does, so the graph is actually re-driven instead of the session sitting at `RUNNING` with no workflow; Terminate persists STOPPED *before* the irreversible teardown; all three writes are forced, since RUNNING/PAUSED/STOPPED do not bypass the 5s throttle and the gate write they supersede was force-saved moments earlier. A bare `post_chat` gate persisted by a crash between the service-level write and the typed one can now be upgraded instead of leaving Terminate as the only exit. `SNAPSHOT_NOT_FOUND` maps to 404 regardless of producer class, so an idempotently-retried DELETE is no longer told the store is full. Three endpoints in `interaction.py` could release the fail-closed gate and now refuse with 409: `/human-input`, which signals the same `_continue_event` the round gate waits on; `/supplementary-analysis`, which signals it too through `signal_supplementary_analysis(target='direction'|'round')` and drove a full PI-planned supplementary run — bioinformatics agent, up to 30 kernel steps, workspace and AnnData mutation, report regeneration — past a boundary the design guarantees is snapshotted, so a later successful Retry would capture that work under the label of the boundary it was meant to precede (its guard is placed *before* the target/status branches, since `target='round'` has no status check at all); and `/finalize-from-integration`, which guarded only on `analysis_phase == INTEGRATION and status == WAITING_INPUT` — precisely the two conditions a required-snapshot gate leaves intact, as it parks on `WAITING_INPUT` and never touches `analysis_phase` — and ran the graph to COMPLETED with `snapshot_failure` still armed, no node, callback or `finally` between the endpoint and the report rechecking it.
  - **A terminal restore keeps the reports it is restoring (`backend/services/snapshot_v2/dispatcher.py`)**: `activate` invalidated all four report paths for every capsule kind "so a restore triggers fresh generation", but a terminal restore sets COMPLETED and both `run()` and `resume()` short-circuit before entering the graph — nothing can regenerate them. Restoring the one snapshot that represents the finished analysis left every report view answering 404, with the destructive re-analysis path as the only way back.
  - **Listing reconciliation is additive over V1 records (`backend/services/snapshot_v2/service_parts/queries.py`, `backend/services/session_store.py`)**: `rebuild_snapshots_from_manifests` built its listing from `verify_all_committed()` alone and returned it as the whole of `AnalysisState.snapshots`, so every legacy V1 record — `format_version` null/1, no committed manifest by construction — was dropped the first time `ensure_session_loaded` reconciled, and the next save persisted the loss. The V1 compatibility surface the module contract promises then went dead: `build_legacy_inventory` reported 0 and the legacy DELETE answered 404, leaving sidecars under `sessions/<id>/snapshots/` (which V2 GC never sweeps) reclaimable only by deleting the whole session. The reconcile now carries forward exactly the entries `legacy._is_v1_snapshot` accepts and never a `format_version >= 2` record, so a V2 projection whose manifest is gone still disappears. The baseline comes from a new optional `existing` argument, passed from the load path — the only place the V1 records can be read from — and defaulting to the cached listing so the capture/delete call sites stop re-dropping what the load path kept. The sort key was made tz-tolerant at the same time: V2 projections are aware, persisted V1 records are naive, and Python refuses to order the two.
  - **Export stages where it lands (`backend/services/snapshot_v2/exporter.py`, `backend/routes/export_import_parts/export_routes.py`)**: the V2 archive is now built in `UPLOAD_TEMP_DIR`, so publishing it is a rename rather than a multi-GB cross-device copy — and that move is off the event loop regardless.
  - **A runtime-omitted export can be imported (`backend/services/snapshot_v2/exporter.py`, `importer.py`)**: `include_runtime=false` left the runtime object *files* out but wrote each manifest verbatim, still declaring every runtime hash — and the importer verified the raw manifests before the plan could strip those refs, so it answered MISSING_OBJECT. The documented "base objects always export, runtime only with include_runtime=True" archive was unusable on every installation including the one that produced it, and the import fixtures never caught it because they pack the union of all manifest hashes regardless of the flag they stamp. The exporter now emits a manifest describing what it actually packed (runtime downgraded to `missing`, root_hash recomputed), and verification runs over `plan.manifests` so archives already produced by earlier builds import too.
  - **Import rolls back to invisible, never to half-published (`backend/services/snapshot_v2/importer.py`, `deletion.py`)**: a failure between `mark_pending_imports` and `clear_pending_imports` used to leave both the markers and the manifests already published on disk, permanently invisible and un-reclaimable with every retry re-failing at the same point, so both handlers now roll back. Rolling back correctly needs two more things. It must clear only the markers whose manifests it actually removed: dropping a marker is what makes a manifest visible, and the callee's store-wide `verify_all_committed()` was *guaranteed* to raise before touching a file on the dominant path into the rollback — `IMPORT_RECONCILE_CORRUPT`, raised by `reconcile()` over that same visible set — so an import the API answered with 422 published its snapshots: listable, loadable, restorable, quota-consuming, with no `ImportBatchRecord` behind them, and unrepairable because recovery declines to act on a FAILED import journal. `_rollback_pending_publish` now takes the `DeleteResult` and clears nothing when the delete raised, and the delete no longer verifies unrelated manifests, since its set is authorized by markers, not by the health of the store. And it must reclaim objects through the shared mark phase: `delete_pending_import_manifests` hand-rolled its reachability set from `verify_all_committed()`, which by construction excludes `audit-*` bundles, the other pending-import manifests and journal-declared hashes — the three root classes `SnapshotGarbageCollector.mark()` protects — so because objects are content-addressed and a pending manifest routinely declares a hash the destination already held, the unlink loop reached through it and destroyed the last reference held by an audit bundle. The unlink loop is gone; the rollback removes the manifest, signature and marker and then calls `self.gc.run()` exactly as `_delete_impl` does, best-effort since it runs inside an exception handler and must not turn a completed rollback into a raised one.
  - **Import bounds its reads before they allocate, and honours its configured limits (`backend/services/snapshot_v2/importer.py`, `object_store.py`, `service.py`, `backend/routes/export_import_parts/import_routes.py`)**: `getmembers()` materialised a TarInfo for every member before `max_member_count` could run, so the limit could not protect the allocation it exists for; the member list is now built lazily and capped as it grows. Every regular member counts toward `max_uncompressed_bytes` (it counted only `objects/`), and the two members read whole into memory — the export metadata and each manifest — get their own caps and bounded reads, instead of being governed only by the 80 GB single-member limit. `import_snapshots` builds the importer with `SnapshotImportLimits.from_config` so the operator's `snapshot_v2_import_*` settings reach the one path they exist for, and two of those settings then needed an actual consumer: `session_quota_bytes` and `free_space_reserve_bytes` were declared and populated but read by nobody, so the only ceilings pass 2 respected were per-archive and successive imports could take a session past the operator's quota with no cumulative bound and drive the shared data volume to zero free space. A single admission check now runs after `build_import_plan` and before pass 2 allocates, projecting only the hashes the store does not already hold — so an idempotent re-import that adds nothing is not rejected — and probing `disk_usage` with the same walk-up `_check_quota` uses; it has to sit after the plan, not in `_check_member_limits`, which sees raw members rather than the sanitized set. `import_v2` likewise streamed the request body into `UPLOAD_TEMP_DIR` comparing `buffer_size` only against 0 and evaluated `max_compressed_bytes` from `stat().st_size`, i.e. after the entire body was already on the volume that carries every session's metadata; the ceiling is now read once before the copy loop and 413 raised the moment it is passed.
  - **Legacy imports release the session id they claimed (`backend/routes/export_import_parts/import_impl.py`, `import_routes.py`)**: the zip and JSON paths call the same allocator as tar.gz — which takes ownership by creating `sessions/<id>/` — but had none of its cleanup. A truncated archive or a ValidationError left an ownerless directory with no `session.json`: invisible in the UI, never reclaimed, and holding the archive's own id forever, so a corrected re-upload silently landed under a fresh uuid.
  - **A partial `state_sync` no longer blanks the session, and a commit the panel missed still refreshes it (`frontend/src/hooks/api/useWebSocket.ts`, `frontend/src/utils/transformLoadResponse.ts`, `frontend/src/components/SnapshotPanel.tsx`)**: `updateSession` spreads its argument, so a key present with value `undefined` erases the real value — and the restore job's transaction sync carries no `status`, `current_round` or `version`. Every restore progress event blanked the analysis control bar and disabled version-conflict detection. `stopped` was also missing from `VALID_STATUSES`, so a terminated session reloaded as `pending` with an enabled Start button. Snapshot delete now surfaces the backend's 409 instead of an unhandled rejection. The same handler's post-commit refresh was gated on `info.reconnect === true`, a field no backend producer emits — `SnapshotTransactionRecord` has no such key — so the branch was dead, and since that sync deliberately carries no status fields the partial-update block below it wrote nothing either: a restore committing while `SnapshotPanel` was unmounted (reachable, since the page can be reloaded mid-restore and the panel only auto-reopens for a retry transaction) left the client rendering pre-restore state against an already-rewritten server. The dead condition is replaced by a `/session/{id}/load` refetch — idempotent, and it does not tear down a live socket the way a reconnect would — through the same transform path the version-conflict branch uses, deduped per transaction id so the reconnect replay cannot loop it.
  - **The snapshot panel polls, respects its own cap, and can be closed (`frontend/src/components/SnapshotPanel.tsx`, `AnalysisPanel.tsx`)**: the polling effect depended on `startPolling`, whose identity chains to the inline `onRestore` prop, so it tore down on every parent render and its cleanup killed the interval `beginRestore` had just started — the panel's own poller never ran once. Polling is now driven by a state-held transaction id with the handler read through a ref. The parent's retry monitor stops at 150 x 2 s, exactly the backend's 300 s safe-point timeout, and no longer stops silently — but the give-up write defeated that cap: writing `poll_timed_out` through `setSnapshotTransaction` allocates a new object, the selector saw a fresh reference, and the monitor effect, whose dependency array contained the very record its poller rewrites, re-ran; because `stopSnapshotRetryPolling()` had already nulled the ref the re-entry guard could not fire and a fresh `attempts: 0` restarted the loop (measured: 201 requests in 200 ticks). The same structural cause made the auto-open effect level-triggered on a record rewritten every 2 s, so it re-asserted `setShowSnapshotPanel(true)` on every poll and the panel's X was inoperative for the whole life of a retry restore — a `fixed inset-0 z-50` modal returning within a tick of every close, and because of the cap defect for the entire restore rather than a bounded 300 s. Both effects now key off the scalars that actually decide whether they should run, and the open edge is claimed once per transaction; the terminal guard was widened from `committed` to the full terminal set, so a failed or cancelled retry no longer pops the modal back up.
  - **The snapshot UI shows the runtime it has, and marks a corrupt manifest corrupt (`frontend/src/types/snapshotV2.ts`, `components/snapshots/*`, `components/SnapshotPanel.tsx`)**: both the tree chip and the preview block read `manifest.runtime_dependencies`, a key the serialized manifest never contains (it exists only on `SnapshotCaptureRequest`), and the declared TypeScript shape did not match the backend's either — so the blocks were permanently dead and would have crashed if ever populated. Retyped to the authoritative `runtime` manifest; the tree now shows the runtime status, which is what decides whether a restore bounces into the reset/keep dialog. `normalizeSnapshotRecordV2` likewise computes a `SnapshotFailureRecord` for every corrupt V2 entry that nothing read, so a committed manifest failing HMAC/schema/root-hash verification — which `list_snapshots` keeps in the listing on purpose — rendered as an ordinary snapshot: an "Idle" chip, "Invalid Date", "0 files", blank root hash, indistinguishable from a healthy idle one, with Restore offered. The tree now renders the failure code and message in place of the synthesised fields and sorts unusable records last through an `orderKey` helper instead of letting `new Date('')` produce a NaN comparator; the preview returns a dedicated corrupt block instead of reporting a captured time, branch, generation and root hash never read off disk; and Restore is disabled for such a record, since the read-only preflight refuses it with 422 regardless. Delete stays enabled — it is the only way to clear a corrupt entry out of the lineage.
  - **A refused Cancel says so (`frontend/src/components/SnapshotPanel.tsx`)**: `handleCancelTxn` swallowed every rejection into `console.error`, and because the throw lands on the first await the follow-up refresh never ran either, so a refused Cancel produced nothing but a re-enabled button — the same gap `handleDelete` had. Mirrored that path, with the banner at `z-[70]` so it sits above the progress dialog's own overlay, next to the button that produced it.
  - **New tests (`backend/tests/test_snapshot_v2_edge_hardening.py`, `test_snapshot_v2_edge2_{recovery,pre_restore,safepoint,storage,boundary}.py`, `frontend/src/components/__tests__/SnapshotEdge2.test.tsx`, `SnapshotPanel.restore-polling.test.tsx`, `frontend/src/hooks/__tests__/useApi.state-sync-partial.test.ts`)**: 91 backend cases and 12 frontend cases, each verified to fail against the source as it stood before the pass that added it and to pass after. `test_snapshot_v2_gc.py::test_mark_audit_manifests_excluded` is replaced by `test_mark_protects_audit_manifest_objects`; `test_snapshot_v2_import.py::test_import_runtime_omitted_from_archive` now asserts the behaviour its own docstring describes — both pinned the defect — and `test_retire_closes_the_checkpoint_connection_and_evicts` registers a live run task, with a companion case for the no-run leak. Suite: 3467 backend passed / 79 skipped, 229 frontend passed.
  - **Method**: two passes. The first worked outward from the restore transaction; the second re-audited the whole of `snapshot_v2/` plus its workflow, route and frontend surface — deliberately including everything the first pass had just changed — across ten subsystem reviews, and every candidate it produced was then re-verified against the source by an independent reviewer instructed to refute by default. Of that second pass's 30 candidates, nineteen were confirmed as reported and eleven were narrowed (a milder outcome, a tighter trigger, or a different defect at the same site) and fixed as narrowed rather than as claimed; none were fixed on the strength of the original report alone. Six of them were regressions the first pass had introduced or newly exposed, which is why the two are recorded as one version.
  - **Reviewed and deliberately not changed**: the `_restore_resume_node` re-route (`WorkflowState` declares no such channel, so LangGraph drops it before `initialize_round` ever sees it — verified empirically and pinned by a test); `ManifestStore.reconcile()` racing a capture (every caller holds the session lock the capture holds — the dot-prefix filter was added anyway so reconcile is safe on its own terms rather than on its callers'); the un-persisted `upgrade_legacy_snapshot_failure_gate` repair on WebSocket reconnect (cache eviction force-saves, and the upgrade is re-derived on every reconnect); `compute_protected_snapshot_ids`' unfiltered fold of `export_protection` envelopes (recovery now reaps the orphans that made it wrong, and filtering there would open a window where a snapshot an unreaped journal names becomes deletable); `_snapshot_transaction_in_flight`'s inability to distinguish a snapshot transaction from an ordinary route handler holding the same lock (a design change, not a defect — the deferral it causes is now honoured correctly on all three branches); and the unread `poll_timed_out` flag, whose only consumer would be a dialog outside this pass's scope.

- `0.36.11` — The 0.36.10 round trip is verified on the configuration it is named for: Snapshot V2 gate parametrized, continuation checked at the dispatch rather than at the endpoint that only records intent, the two continuation paths no combination reached, frontend refusal handling; version bump (2026-07-29):
  - **The round trip runs under both settings of the Snapshot V2 gate (`backend/tests/test_e2e_http_roundtrip.py`)**: new `snapshot_v2` fixture, applied to every case. The root `conftest.py` sets `SCAI_ENABLE_SNAPSHOT_V2` with `setdefault`, so the whole suite runs on the *enabled* path while production defaults to disabled — meaning the configuration 0.36.10 is named for, the only one on which `/run` and `/resume` call `normalize_disabled_snapshot_state`, was the one configuration its round trip never ran under. The gate is a module global read at import time, so the fixture patches `_config.SNAPSHOT_V2_ENABLED` rather than the environment.
  - **The continuation is checked where it is decided (`backend/routes/websockets.py`, `backend/tests/test_e2e_http_roundtrip.py`)**: the dispatch in `websocket_endpoint` is extracted as `should_resume_workflow` (behaviour unchanged) so tests call the real branch instead of restating its condition. The previous assertions stopped at `POST /run` answering 200 — an endpoint that only records intent and hands back a WebSocket URL — and then read back the status `/run` had itself just written, which says nothing about whether anything would execute. A kernel-less continuation is now checked to reach `initialize_round` with `preserve_round_on_initialize`, i.e. to redo the imported round rather than advance past it and lose it.
  - **Two continuation paths that no combination reached (`backend/tests/test_e2e_http_roundtrip.py`)**: every case continued through `POST /run`, which arms `restore_start_intent` itself and therefore cannot tell whether the import did — deleting `_arm_restore_intent_after_import` left the suite green. Resume on a re-imported runtime archive is where that arming is load-bearing: the id collides, the checkpoint is dropped, the kernel survives, so the runtime reads `complete`, Re-run is not rendered, and Resume — which sets no intent of its own — is the only control. Separately, exporting between Start and the first round completing leaves `current_round` at 0, where both import fixups return early and neither Start nor Re-run renders, so Resume must dispatch a fresh `run()` instead of holding.
  - **The frontend gate is tested on the half only a refusal reaches (`frontend/src/hooks/__tests__/useApi.resume-gate.test.ts`, `frontend/src/components/__tests__/AnalysisPanel.imported-session-controls.test.tsx`)**: the existing helper hard-coded `success: true`, so the `success === false` half of the guard — the only signal three of the four refusals carry — never executed. A `refusedResponse` shape covers it, including that a refusal must not reconnect the WebSocket, since the handler on the other end dispatches the run the backend just declined. A new panel test pins which control an imported session gets in each runtime state: `unavailable` disables Resume and offers Re-run, `complete` offers Resume and renders no Re-run at all.
  - **Still uncovered, recorded rather than implied**: the graph executing for real — every assertion stops at the entry decision, because a step past it needs a live kernel and an LLM — and the round dialog's Continue and Send paths through `blockForwardResume`, which are driven by component state set from WebSocket events.

- `0.36.10` — Imported sessions can actually be continued with Snapshot V2 disabled: `states/` filename rebinding, unusable-checkpoint handling, honest runtime and status marking, recovery-block release, export save durability, json-import session allocation; version bump (2026-07-29):
  - **`states/` members are renamed after the destination session (`backend/routes/export_import_parts/import_impl.py`)**: new `_rebind_states_member_name`, applied in the `states/` branch of `_resolve_import_dest`. `states/` is the one archive root whose session id lives in the *filename* rather than in a path segment the caller strips — `uploads/`, `workspace/` and `reports/` were already rebound, `states/` was not. An import that had to allocate a fresh id (any re-import of a backup on the same installation) therefore wrote `<archive id>.ipynb` and `<archive id>_session.pkl` into the new session, where nothing ever looks: **the analysis notebook was silently lost on the default export path**, and the kernel pkl sat next to a `_live_pkl` that reported no kernel. The suffix must begin at a `.` or `_` boundary so a hand-built archive declaring a short id cannot rewrite an unrelated file.
  - **An unusable checkpoint is dropped rather than imported (`backend/routes/export_import_parts/import_impl.py`)**: new `_is_checkpoint_member` (case-folded, covers `-wal`/`-shm`/`-journal`) skips `checkpoints.sqlite` whenever the import allocated a new id. Every row is keyed by `thread_id`, which is the session id of the run that wrote it, and the blobs carry a serialized `AnalysisState` holding the same id, so it cannot be renamed into place. Left behind, the file satisfied the `has_checkpoint` test in `run_analysis` while holding nothing this session could resume from — which withheld the `explicit_start` intent and made Start execute nothing.
  - **A checkpoint only counts together with its kernel (`backend/routes/lifecycle.py`)**: `has_checkpoint` became `has_resumable_checkpoint`, requiring `checkpoints.sqlite` *and* the live pkl. `include_runtime=true` puts the checkpoint in the archive unconditionally while the pkl needs `trust=true` on the way in, and the UI asks those two questions separately — so "checkpoint without kernel" is reachable from ordinary use, and it used to send Re-run into an exact continuation on an empty kernel instead of a fresh round.
  - **The restore intent is armed at import time, not by one button (`backend/routes/export_import_parts/import_impl.py`)**: new `_arm_restore_intent_after_import` writes `restore_start_intent="explicit_start"` when an already-executed session arrives without a resumable checkpoint. `POST /run` set it, but the frontend only renders that button while `isRerunEnabled()` is true. An import that carried a kernel under a reallocated id has its runtime marked `complete`, so the only reachable control was Resume — which never set the intent — and `resume()` held without executing anything on every attempt.
  - **Runtime availability reads only the live pkl (`backend/routes/export_import_parts/import_impl.py`)**: `wrote_sandbox_pkl` is now `is_pkl_member and dest_path == _live_pkl(session_id)`. `is_sandbox_pickle` also matches per-snapshot `<snap_id>_session.pkl.zst` backups, so an archive carrying only those made a session with no live kernel claim it had one, bypassing the `is_forward_resume_blocked` gate that exists to prevent exactly that.
  - **A recovery block can be released once Snapshot V2 is off (`backend/services/snapshot_v2/feature_gate.py`)**: `normalize_disabled_snapshot_state` now also clears `recovery_blocked` and `snapshot_transaction.recovery_error`, on a path independent of the snapshot-failure gate so a session carrying only a recovery block keeps its status (the status reconciliation derives from `retry_context.previous_status`, which such a session does not have, and would have downgraded a finished session to paused). The flag is only ever set by journal recovery, which runs only while V2 is enabled, while `assert_recovery_clear` refuses every start, resume, WebSocket attach and kernel launch — so with V2 off nothing could clear it and every snapshot endpoint answers 404. An operator disabling the subsystem to stop the bleeding, or an archive carrying the flag, produced a permanently unstartable session.
  - **Export saves the state it is about to pack (`backend/routes/export_import_parts/export_routes.py`, `backend/routes/sessions.py`)**: the pre-export save and `POST /session/{id}/save` pass `force=True`. `RUNNING` is not in `_FORCE_SAVE_STATUSES`, so the 5-second throttle silently discarded the write that both endpoints perform precisely to capture the latest state; the packer then read a stale `session.json` off disk. A `current_round` still sitting at 0 that way also makes `_mark_runtime_availability_after_import` return before marking anything, producing an archive that claims a kernel it does not carry. `POST /run` and the COMPLETED re-analysis branch of `/resume` force their writes for the same reason.
  - **An imported session does not claim a run nobody owns (`backend/routes/export_import_parts/import_impl.py`)**: new `_normalize_imported_status` lands `RUNNING` and `GENERATING_REPORT` imports on `PAUSED`. Nothing here owns that run — no workflow object, no kernel, no WebSocket — and the frontend reads the status literally: `running` hides the controls that would start one (including the re-run button a runtime-less import depends on), and `generating_report` matches no branch at all, so the session renders with no controls whatsoever.
  - **Re-analysis of a completed session tells the agent its kernel is empty (`backend/routes/lifecycle.py`)**: the COMPLETED branch of `/resume` sets `runtime_recompute_pending` when it clears an `unavailable` runtime, matching what `/run` already did. Without it `_build_data_context` omits the "KERNEL STATE IS EMPTY" brief and the bioinformatics agent proceeds as though `adata` were still loaded.
  - **The json import path creates a session like the others (`backend/routes/export_import_parts/import_routes.py`)**: it now goes through `_allocate_import_session_id` with the same remapping and rebinding as tar.gz and zip. It was the only path that took the id straight from the uploaded document and then stamped the caller's `user_id` onto it, so posting `{"id": "<another user's session>"}` handed that session over.
  - **The frontend stops marking refused requests as running (`frontend/src/hooks/api/useSession.ts`, `frontend/src/components/AnalysisPanel.tsx`)**: `resumeAnalysis` and `continueRound` honour `success === false` and `data.runtime_unavailable` — four refusals reach them (runtime unavailable, recovery blocked, snapshot failure, pending plan review) and only the first carries a flag. A new shared `blockForwardResume` guards all three entry points that advance the analysis; previously only Resume checked, so the round dialog's Continue and Send sent a request the backend refused and then set `status: 'running'`, which hides the re-run button behind `!isRunning` and left a page reload as the only way back. The guard leaves the dialog mounted so the user's typed message and file selection survive. The snapshot retry poll now stops and corrects the capability flag on `SNAPSHOT_V2_DISABLED` instead of retrying a guaranteed 404 until the tab closes, and the runtime banner no longer points at a `/rerun` endpoint that does not exist.
  - **Startup says when it steps over restore journals (`backend/main.py`)**: with V2 disabled, `_warn_about_unrecovered_journals` reports sessions holding a `STAGED`/`STAGING`/`APPLYING` journal (or an unreadable one). A crash during the APPLYING stage leaves the tree half-swapped and the journal describing how to finish or roll back; recovery genuinely cannot run without the object store, but the operator should learn it from a log line rather than from the results. Terminal journals are excluded — they are merely waiting for the next enabled startup to reap them.
  - **New tests (`backend/tests/test_export_import_hardening.py`, `backend/tests/test_export_import_routes_hardening.py`, `backend/tests/test_snapshot_v2_feature_gate.py`, `backend/tests/test_e2e_http_roundtrip.py`, `frontend/src/hooks/__tests__/useApi.resume-gate.test.ts`)**: `states/` rebinding and its boundary conditions, checkpoint dropping and retention, snapshot-pkl runtime marking, the armed restore intent, json-import id allocation, imported status normalization, recovery-block release without status damage, the checkpoint-plus-kernel test, and the resume-refusal handling. `test_e2e_http_roundtrip.py` drives the whole round trip — create, populate, export, import, continue — through the real routes and auth for four export/import combinations; it is what surfaced the throttled pre-export save that no unit-level check could reach.

- `0.36.9` — Session export/import hardening: import path confinement, session-id collision, portable-archive resumability, export authentication, link-member archives, endpoint/credential pairing, anchored path remapping, failure cleanup; version bump (2026-07-29):
  - **Import destinations confined to the session directory (`backend/routes/export_import_parts/import_impl.py`)**: `_resolve_import_dest` is now a whitelist that returns `None` for any archive path outside the session-centric layout, and the caller skips those members; the destination guard tightened from `validate_path_within(dest, DATA_BASE_DIR)` to `validate_path_within(dest, get_session_dir(session_id))`. The old catch-all (`return DATA_BASE_DIR / relative_to_base`) let any authenticated user place an archive member anywhere under the data root — `app.db` (the account and API-key store), `.jwt_secret` (the token signing key) and other users' `sessions/<id>/session.json` were all writable, and the write happened before the "session state JSON not found" check, so even a rejected import completed the damage. The product's own reproducibility bundle proved the branch was live code: it is emitted at the archive root and landed in a global `<data>/reproducibility/` shared by every import. `logs/` and `reproducibility/` now map into the session; the bundle goes to the workspace where the generator expects it.
  - **Import creates a session, never overwrites one (`backend/routes/export_import_parts/import_impl.py`)**: New `_allocate_import_session_id` / `_claim_session_id` take the archive's id only when free, claiming it atomically with `mkdir(exist_ok=False)`; on collision the archive is materialized under a fresh uuid and every embedded path is rebound (`_remap_paths_in_text` rewrites the session-directory prefix, `_rebind_session_id_values` rewrites standalone id values, `state_data["id"]` is reset). Previously the destination came entirely from the archive and nothing checked whether that session already existed — so re-importing a backup on the same installation overwrote the live session's `session.json` while leaving newer workspace files orphaned, and `state_data["user_id"] = user_id` handed another user's session to whoever imported it.
  - **Runtime availability reads what this import wrote (`backend/routes/export_import_parts/import_impl.py`)**: `_mark_runtime_availability_after_import` gained an `imported_pkl` argument. It previously answered "is there a pkl on disk", so a pkl belonging to an earlier run at the destination made an imported session look resumable against a kernel that does not match its state.
  - **Imported sessions can be run again (`backend/routes/lifecycle.py`, `backend/models/schemas.py`, `backend/agents/bioinformatics_agent.py`, `backend/workflow/agents.py`)**: `POST /run` now sets `restore_start_intent="explicit_start"` when the session has no checkpoint and has already run, which routes `resume()` through `_invoke_restore_entry` instead of its "never invent a fresh initialize_round run" fall-through — that fall-through logged, flipped the status back to `PAUSED` and executed nothing, so Start/Re-run on an imported session was a silent no-op with no way forward at all. New one-shot `AnalysisState.runtime_recompute_pending`, set when the run is clearing an `unavailable` runtime, makes `_build_data_context` tell the agent its kernel is empty and to reload the input files; the bioinformatics node clears it after the first run.
  - **Session archives require authentication (`backend/middleware/auth.py`, `backend/routes/export_import_parts/export_routes.py`, `backend/routes/export_import_parts/core.py`, `frontend/src/components/Sidebar.tsx`)**: `/export` and `/export/prepare` are no longer auth-optional; only `/export/download` is, and it now requires an unguessable one-shot token (`secrets.token_urlsafe(32)`) that is bound to the session it was minted for and is not consumed by a wrong-session request. `require_session_ownership` degrades to an existence check when no token is presented, so listing `/export` made a whole-session archive — every upload, figure, report and message — downloadable by anyone who learned the session id from a deliberately auth-optional report URL. The `v1-<session_id>` prepared-export key was derivable from the same id.
  - **Export emits no link members (`backend/routes/export_import_parts/tar_builders.py`, `export_routes.py`)**: archives are built with `dereference=True` and each member is created from an already-open descriptor. Import rejects every SYMTYPE/LNKTYPE member as its first check, so a single `os.symlink` or `cp -l` left in a workspace by analysis code produced an archive that could never be imported by ScAI at all.
  - **Export tolerates a session that is still running (`backend/routes/export_import_parts/tar_builders.py`, `backend/utils/sandbox_parts/shared.py`)**: new `_safe_add_file` skips a file that vanishes or cannot be read instead of aborting the export with a 500, and `_PaddedReader` serves exactly the announced size so a file that shrinks mid-pack cannot raise inside `copyfileobj` and leave a structurally broken archive. The live notebook — rewritten in place on every save and packed into every archive — is now written atomically via a temp file plus `os.replace`.
  - **Endpoints never borrow the installation credential (`backend/utils/secret_redaction.py`, `backend/utils/settings_provider.py`, `backend/utils/agent_llm.py`)**: `strip_analysis_state_secrets` now blanks endpoint fields (`llm_base_url`, `base_url`, `deep_research_llm_base_url`, `proxy_url`) alongside the credentials, and both LLM client factories fall back to the global client when an agent has a custom endpoint but no key of its own. Export stripped `api_key` and kept `llm_base_url`, so an imported session built a client pairing the archive's endpoint with the destination's key — every call failed, and a hand-edited archive could name any host.
  - **`config_io` writes the endpoint key the runtime reads (`backend/services/config_io.py`)**: `build_agent_server_configs` emits `llm_base_url`, not `base_url`. Both consumers read `llm_base_url`, so an imported configuration silently lost its endpoint while keeping the credential that belonged to it, sending that credential to the destination's global provider.
  - **Path remapping is anchored and blob-aware (`backend/routes/export_import_parts/import_impl.py`, `backend/config/_config.py`)**: `_remap_paths_in_text` replaces the source data root only at a delimiter followed by `/`, refuses a relative root rather than guessing, and now also runs over the blob-inlined state. `DATA_BASE_DIR` is resolved to an absolute path. An unanchored `str.replace` of the Docker image's `/data` rewrote every `https://host/data/...` URL and `/dataset/...` literal in the document, and strings ≥ 1 KB (code history, report segments) live in byte-copied `blobs/*.b64` sidecars that the first remap never saw.
  - **Archive member filtering is segment-wise (`backend/routes/export_import_parts/import_impl.py`)**: new `_is_unsafe_member_name` rejects a `..` path *segment* instead of the substring. The old test silently dropped ordinary analysis output such as `DEG_A..B.csv`, so the restored session was missing files the archive contained and the job still reported success.
  - **Nothing is written before the state validates (`backend/routes/export_import_parts/import_impl.py`)**: `AnalysisState` is constructed before `session.json` is written, and `_process_import_job` now cleans up the session it created on every failure path — the previous cleanup was unreachable because its guard variable was assigned only after the extraction returned, while cancellation is raised inside it. A payload this build cannot load used to leave a directory that `list_saved_sessions` shows and `load_session_from_disk` refuses, producing a sidebar entry that could be neither opened nor deleted. Unknown manifest versions are warned about, and a manifest whose `session_id` disagrees with the archive directory is rejected.
  - **Import jobs are scoped to their owner, and extraction stops on a full disk (`backend/routes/export_import_parts/import_routes.py`, `backend/routes/export_import_parts/core.py`)**: job records carry `user_id`; status and cancel report 404 for anyone else, and a client-chosen `X-Import-Id` cannot take over a live job (409). `MIN_FREE_DISK_BYTES` / `free_disk_bytes` abort extraction with 507 instead of filling the data volume.
  - **Prepared exports are unique and reclaimed (`backend/routes/export_import_parts/export_routes.py`, `backend/tasks/session_cleanup.py`, `backend/main.py`, `backend/config/_config.py`)**: `/export/prepare` writes to a unique temp path — the previous path was derived from the session id and name alone, so a second prepare truncated the archive the first was still writing — and new `_cleanup_stale_prepared_exports` deletes archives nobody downloaded after `PREPARED_EXPORT_TTL`.
  - **Export filename cap counts bytes (`backend/routes/export_import_parts/core.py`)** and **the upload header is percent-encoded (`frontend/src/components/Sidebar.tsx`, `backend/routes/export_import_parts/import_routes.py`)**: `slugify_session_name` keeps CJK, so a ~78-character Chinese session name exceeded the filesystem's 255-*byte* limit and made that session permanently un-exportable, while `xhr.setRequestHeader('X-Filename', file.name)` threw outright on any non-Latin-1 name — every session named in Chinese produced an archive its own UI could not re-upload.
  - **Both export endpoints share one builder (`backend/routes/export_import_parts/export_routes.py`)**: new `_build_session_tar` replaces two byte-identical copies that nonetheless stamped different manifest versions (`1.0` vs `2.0`); `ARCHIVE_FORMAT_VERSION` is now the single source. LLM interaction logs (`sessions/<id>/logs/`) are packed, so a migrated session's regenerated report no longer claims every agent made zero calls.
  - **Remaining import paths caught up (`backend/routes/export_import_parts/import_routes.py`, `import_impl.py`)**: the legacy zip path extracts uploads, reports and blob sidecars (not only the notebook and workspace) and applies the same remapping; the plain-JSON path inlines blobs and marks runtime availability; extension dispatch is case-insensitive and an unsupported format returns 400 instead of a bare 500 from `json.loads`.
  - **Chat delegation honours the runtime gate (`backend/routes/chat_parts/delegation.py`)**: bioinformatics delegation refuses when `is_forward_resume_blocked`, instead of starting an empty sandbox while its own prompt asserts every earlier result is loaded.
  - **Runtime warning is shown with Snapshot V2 off (`frontend/src/components/AnalysisPanel.tsx`)**: the banner was gated on `snapshotV2Enabled`, but an ordinary import sets `runtime_restore_status='unavailable'` with the subsystem disabled, leaving Resume disabled with the explanation suppressed.
  - **`ConfigV1` describes the sessions the product creates (`backend/models/config_schema.py`)**: `IndicatorEntry.unit` is optional (the PI agent writes `""` when a document states none) and the name/key length caps were dropped, since nothing bounds those values where they are set — the caps only made an existing session's configuration permanently un-exportable with a 500.
  - **New tests (`backend/tests/test_export_import_hardening.py`, `backend/tests/test_export_import_routes_hardening.py`)**: 42 cases covering import confinement, id collision and rebinding, stale-pkl runtime marking, segment-wise member filtering, anchored and blob-aware remapping, validate-before-write, link-free archives, log packing, byte-capped filenames, export authentication and token binding, prepared-export reclamation, job ownership, the disk-space floor, explicit-start re-run, the recompute prompt, endpoint/credential pairing, and the `config_io` key contract.
- `0.36.8-rc7` — A completeness gate that was dead from the day it was written; rc6's terminal-round trigger was dead from the day IT was written, twice over; the user-facing supplementary path raised on every target; and a proposed expansion criterion measured and rejected (2026-07-30):
  - **The evidence-binding keys never survived the node's result dict, so a gate written in 0.34.9 had never once run.** `_bioinformatics_analysis` builds a fixed-key `final_result`, and `git show 7401f05:backend/workflow/agents.py` confirms the gate shipped alongside a six-key literal that already omitted `evidence_verified` / `evidence_execution_ids`. That dict is the only writer of workflow-state `bioinformatics_result` on the normal path (`workflow/agents.py`; the other two writers are the cache-hit and waiting-data early returns), so three consumers that all test `is False` saw `None` forever: the completeness gate in `derive_round_completion_status`, which withholds `direction_coverage_eligible` from a round whose findings are not bound to successful executions; the evidence note handed to the PI at synthesis; and the report hedging at `services/report/planning.py:207,244` plus `agents/supervisor/report_content.py:331,361`. Corroboration: `bioinformatics_evidence_verified` is `None` and `evidence_execution_ids` empty for all ~60 round results across the 9 sessions on disk (0.34.5-0.36.8-rc3). The keys are now carried, and the evidence note is three-state — `None` means the binding was never recorded and must read as unverified, where the old two-branch `is False` form told the PI "Findings are linked to successful execution records" on every round however degraded. **Blast radius, checked rather than assumed**: `report_findings` is hard-rejected with `continue` when its cited ids are empty or not from this run's successful executions (`agents/bioinformatics_agent.py:2560-2582`), so a clean completion always has ids; and `evidence_gate_degraded` forces `status="partial"` (:3573), which already reached ineligible via the `has_core_partial` branch. Running the real `derive_round_completion_status` over every emittable `(status, evidence_verified)` pair, with and without the keys, changes exactly one row — `paused_for_revision` — and `_after_bioinformatics` routes that to `plan_revision` before `pi_synthesis` is reached. So the fix makes a dead gate reachable without changing any live outcome; its real value is the note and the hedging. Old sessions and restored snapshots are unaffected: their persisted `None` still fails every `is False` test.
  - **rc6's terminal-round trigger never fired, for two independent reasons, and the first was a documented repeat.** `directions_queue` is popped one round LATE — `_pi_planning` peeks `directions_queue[0]` with an explicit "do NOT pop the queue here" comment and `_initialize_round` pops it at the start of the NEXT round — so the entry for the direction a round is running is still queued while it runs, and rc6's bare `if directions_queue: return False` guard fired on every last direction. `docs/HANDOVER.md` records `0.30.4` fixing this exact trap for the multi-direction terminal gate and adding `_multi_direction_analysis_completed` to compensate; rc6 reintroduced the criterion 0.30.4 removed. The second reason was missed in the first diagnosis and is why fixing only the queue would have looked correct in a two-direction unit test while leaving the trigger dead: the integration branch sets `max_rounds = current_round + len(selected_dirs)` (`workflow/planning.py:438`), one round more than the batch it plans — the spare slot being the integration round itself — so `current_round >= max_rounds` was false on every round of every integration batch, empty queue included. In the shape all 9 recorded sessions ran (2-direction exploration batch, then 1-direction integration batches) that accounts for half the innovation rounds. Both tests are gone.
  - **"Terminal round" is not decidable at that point, so the signal no longer claims to be.** Whether any analysis round follows depends on `integration_synthesis` incrementing `integration_cycle` (`workflow/integration.py:77`) and the PI then choosing how many new directions to open — both after the bioinformatics node has run. rc6's docstring called the predicate "deliberately conservative: it must be certain, not likely", which dressed an unanswerable question as caution. `_is_terminal_analysis_round` is replaced by `_is_last_direction_round_of_batch`, which asks the one thing that IS decidable: is a direction other than the running one queued. It also filters the in-flight entry because the two queue writers disagree on whether it is present — `_pi_planning` stores `selected_dirs[1:]` while `routes/interaction.py:292` stores every chosen direction including the running one, so a user who picks exactly one direction previously never reached the trigger either. Verified against all five state shapes the real nodes produce; three of the four that should fire were returning False before. **Behaviour change to note when reading run logs**: the signal now fires once per direction batch rather than once per session — about three times per session in the recorded shape — still bounded by `post_execution_review_max_tasks` and sharing that budget with the repair signals.
  - **The user-facing supplementary analysis raised `TypeError` on every target, and had done so long before its round entry point was wired up.** `_initialize_round` seeds every agent-result channel with an explicit `None` (`workflow/initialize.py:230`), and `_current_graph_state` is the bioinformatics node's INPUT dict (`workflow/agents.py:90,320`; the round's real result is returned in a new dict), so `_merge_supplementary_findings` read `self._current_graph_state.get(agent_key, {})` and got `None` — a present-but-`None` key, where the default never applies — then assigned into it. The `isinstance(existing, dict)` check guarded only the read of `findings`. Executed against the real nodes: `'NoneType' object does not support item assignment`, swallowed by the caller's try/except and surfaced as "Supplementary bioinformatics analysis failed" AFTER the analysis had been paid for, with the dialog re-arming to invite an identical retry. This was never a `target="round"` defect: `literature_result` is `None` in the same dict, so the `annotation` and `direction` targets — both shipped long before rc6 — failed identically. One guard fixes three entry points. It had no test; `test_supplementary_deque.py` only checks that a deque is a deque.
  - **Two more defects on that path, both real.** Its executions were dropped: `_run_supplementary_analysis` read only `findings`, never appending `code_history` / `analysis_log` to `analysis_state`, so nothing it ran could be cited as evidence — unlike the round path and unlike rc6's repair pass, which both merge them with identity-based dedup. `_absorb_supplementary_executions` now does the same. And `plan_supplementary_analysis` had only an `annotation` branch and an `else`, so `context="round"` told the PI the checkpoint was "research direction selection" and handed it round number, phase and cluster count — no direction, no `pi_summary`, none of the round's findings. It now has its own branch and receives the round's results.
  - **A proposed expansion criterion was measured and rejected before being built.** The open question behind rc6 was whether a round whose plan completed cleanly should still discuss adding analysis within the same topic. The candidate trigger was mechanism-program `missing_evidence`, attractive because it is structured and populated for 146/146 programs in the recorded corpus. Classifying all 542 items by method vocabulary: 60.5% match nothing, 27.5% match measurement vocabulary, and only 65 (12.0%) match computational vocabulary — of which manual inspection puts roughly 8-12 as actually runnable on data in hand, i.e. **15-20% precision**. The dominant false positive is the "replicate-aware"/"replicated pseudobulk" class, whose feasibility depends on per-sample metadata the run may not have and therefore cannot be decided from wording at all. So the classification ships as `label_evidence_gap` producing `mentions_new_measurement` / `mentions_computation` / `unlabelled` — text features, named so they cannot be mistaken for a feasibility verdict — and `collect_structured_evidence_gaps` records the verbatim items plus any program whose schema-required `evidence_execution_ids` came back empty. It is observation only: kept out of the trigger path AND out of the PI's synthesis context, with a test asserting `evidence_gaps` never appears in `_pi_synthesis`, so recording it cannot change what a round concludes. This is the third time in this release series a first-principles criterion had to be measured before it could be trusted (`0.36.8-rc1`, `0.36.8-rc5`); the difference is that this one was measured first.
  - **One reviewer recommendation was deliberately not taken.** A repair pass that succeeds still leaves a round reporting `max_steps_reached` and ineligible for direction coverage, and it was suggested that `_merge_supplementary_into_round` update `status` and `evidence_verified`. It appends the successful executions to `evidence_execution_ids` — they are citable evidence — and leaves the other two alone. `evidence_verified is False` records that THIS ROUND'S REPORTED FINDINGS were not bound to successful executions; running five more cells afterwards does not bind them. Overwriting it would buy coverage eligibility by erasing the fact that earned the ineligibility, which is precisely the hedging the gate exists to force. The reasoning is now a comment at the site and a paragraph in `AGENTS.md`.
  - **Known and not fixed here.** The expansion question itself is still open, and the honest next step is to point the EXISTING judgement at it: `review_round_execution`'s prompt already states the correct criterion ("against THE DATA ALREADY LOADED IN THIS SESSION'S KERNEL", wet-lab work to `out_of_scope`), so a propose-only mode that stores `verdict` / `supplementary_tasks` / `out_of_scope` without executing would measure the real variable, where `missing_evidence` text could not. Not built here. The severity chain a prior review raised is real but currently theoretical: `pending_integration` is gated on `active_batch_complete`, and `integration_synthesis` is the only node that generates new directions, so one ineligible round does end direction generation for a session — but per the blast-radius check above, this release cannot newly produce an ineligible round. `_is_last_direction_round_of_batch` is an approximation by construction and will fire on batches that are followed by more work. `label_evidence_gap`'s `pseudotime` entry is still half prose, kept because it also appears as an `obs` column name. The duplicated tests in `test_snapshot_v2_runtime_edges.py` that rc5 flagged remain, and another concurrent session's 236-line addition to that same file is again deliberately excluded from this commit.
  - **Verification is by test only; no session has been run on this code**, the same gap rc6 declared. 18 new backend test cases — 8 in the new `test_supplementary_merge.py` (that path had zero coverage: present-but-`None` channels for two agent keys, an absent key, the empty-state short-circuit, executions becoming citable, dedup on re-absorb, dict-shaped log entries, malformed input degrading, and the PI round branch), 8 more in `test_post_execution_wiring.py` (the last-direction predicate across the real state shapes, the two traps it must not fall into, the evidence gate firing and not firing on an otherwise-healthy round, `final_result` carrying the binding keys, `None` not reading as verified, and the evidence-gap observation staying out of the PI prompt), 2 in `test_post_execution_review.py` (the gap labels, and `collect_structured_evidence_gaps` over both program shapes). Four rc6 assertions that pinned the old terminal semantics were rewritten rather than deleted. Full backend suite: 3663 passed, 79 skipped, with the same one pre-existing failure as rc5 and rc6 (`test_citation_integrity.py::test_lfc_shrink_is_documented_with_its_required_argument`), confirmed pre-existing in a detached worktree at rc5. Every claim in this entry was independently re-verified against current code by a reviewer instructed to refute it; that review found the `max_rounds` half of the terminal-round diagnosis, which the first pass had missed.

- `0.36.8-rc6` — A round ended when the executor said it was done and nothing compared that against the approved plan: post-execution plan audit, a gated supplementary pass, and three signals that were already being produced and thrown away (2026-07-30):
  - **Nothing in the system knew what a round had executed.** `PlanStep` declares `status` and `executed_at` (`models/schema_parts/session.py:162-163`) and both have readers — `workflow/callbacks.py:125`, `agents/bioinformatics/shared.py:164`, `agents/bioinformatics/planning.py:627` — and, as of rc5, no writer anywhere in the backend: every `.status =` assignment in the repo is plan-level or session-level. Across the 9 real sessions on disk, `[completed] Step` appears 0 times against 762 `[pending] Step` renderings, and all 9 completed sessions ended with every step of their 32-52 step plans still `pending`, which also silently emptied the "COMPLETED (LOCKED)" block of all 126 revision prompts. The new audit in `backend/workflow/post_execution_review.py` is that missing writer. It matches each approved step against method identifiers found in the round's *successful* executions, with two exemptions that exist because they were the measured false-positive sources: wording the plan itself hedged ("scCODA if available", "propeller if assumptions are reasonable"), and tooling the kernel demonstrably lacks (an `ImportError`/`ModuleNotFoundError` naming the family, not round-filtered, because a package missing in round 1 was missing in round 5). A step with no machine-checkable identifier is recorded `unverifiable` and **never** counted as a gap — conflating "we could not check" with "it did not run" is what made rc5 delete `plan_retention.py`, and this is the narrower thing that survives it: not a coverage ratio over prose, but a binary claim about a named tool.
  - **The supplementary machinery already existed end to end and had no trigger.** `_run_supplementary_analysis` (`workflow/core.py:1058`) plans with the PI, re-enters `run_autonomous_analysis(max_steps=30)`, merges findings back with a `[Supplementary]` tag and regenerates a report segment; `workflow/synthesis.py:999` already drained a `target="round"` request at the post-synthesis gate. It fired **zero** times across all 9 sessions, and it could not have: `frontend/src/components/AnalysisPanel.tsx:578` typed the target as `'annotation' | 'direction'`, so `'round'` was not a value the UI could send, and `RoundFeedbackDialog.tsx` had no affordance for it. Two things were added rather than one — the agent-initiated pass described below, and the missing `'round'` wiring so a human can still ask at the gate (`DEFAULT_ROUND_WAIT_SECONDS` is 300, but all 9 runs set `round_wait_seconds=0`, which short-circuits the entire consumer loop at `synthesis.py:918`; that behaviour is unchanged here).
  - **The trigger cannot be the PI's opinion, and the terminal round is why the feature exists.** Parsing every `synthesize_round` response across the 9 sessions: `should_stop` is False in 39 of 40, and `next_bioinformatics` is never empty (1-15 items, mean 7.4). A loop gated on "does the PI think anything is open" therefore fires every round and has no termination condition. The gate uses hard structural signals only: a non-clean exit status, the agent's own written not-run admissions, an unmet approved step, or the terminal round. That last one is the case with no other signal and the one certain loss: in 9/9 sessions the final synthesis emitted 6/8/6/8/1/7/8/7/7 = 58 `next_bioinformatics` proposals and there are zero `execute_code` calls after it, while the literature agent still ran another 26-46 calls — so it is not resource exhaustion, it is that no round follows. Roughly 93% of those proposals were runnable on data already in the kernel. Note the scope this leaves: `direction_payload.py` (rc4/rc5) already hands the previous round's next steps to the next round's planner, so the mid-run leak is largely a routing problem that is already being fixed; what no version of that mechanism can cover is the terminal round and same-round repair, which is exactly what this pass is limited to.
  - **Placement was chosen to touch no bookkeeping.** The audit and the pass run *inside* `_bioinformatics_analysis` (`workflow/agents.py`, between result collection and PI summarization), not as a graph node, and the pass merges its findings, figures, `code_history` and `analysis_log` into the same `result` dict. So the round still produces exactly one `RoundResult` for one direction, and `max_rounds`, `directions_queue`, `active_direction_batch`, the integration cycle cap and `check_convergence` are untouched — which is why this was preferred over the alternative of letting convergence keep a direction for a second round. A re-audit runs after a merge so the persisted plan reflects the repair rather than the state that triggered it. Measured context for that choice: across 9 sessions and 30 innovation rounds, rounds-per-direction is exactly 1.00 with no `direction_key` ever repeating, and `max_rounds` is computed as the direction count (`planning.py:476`), so a second round on the same topic cannot be budgeted at all.
  - **Two time budgets, because the first attempt used one and it was wrong.** The pass initially had a single 900s wall-clock ceiling covering code execution — a number with nothing behind it. Measured against `execution_time` on all 797 recorded executions: median 1.7s, p90 46.7s, p95 79.4s, p99 278s, max 817.5s, and nothing over 900s — but the slowest cell already consumed 91% of that ceiling, and the ceiling was shared by up to 5 steps, while the supplementary work is precisely the heavy kind (GRN inference, deconvolution, full ligand-receptor aggregation). `post_execution_review_timeout_seconds` now bounds ONE step and defaults (0) to `sandbox_timeout + 120` = 5520s; a test pins the invariant that it must exceed `sandbox_timeout`, because the sandbox — not this budget — is what bounds and interrupts a cell (`utils/sandbox_parts/execution.py:283` calls `interrupt_kernel()` on its own timeout, never on ours). `post_execution_review_total_timeout_seconds` defaults (0) to the sum of the step budgets and is floored at one step, so it backstops a non-converging pass without ever being the thing that cuts a step off. Both budgets now interrupt the kernel themselves: cancelling a `wait_for` only stops *us* waiting, and a dispatched cell would otherwise keep the kernel while the workflow moved on. A step overrun is written back into the executor's transcript so it picks a cheaper route instead of abandoning the pass, and `baseline` is deliberately not re-captured afterwards — an interrupted cell may have left the object half-modified, and the next step's invariant check is what has to catch that.
  - **Three signals were already being produced and dropped on the floor.** `report_findings` carries a `suggestions` array in 73 of 73 real calls and the fixed-key `final_result` dict discarded it, so the PI synthesizing the round never saw what the agent that ran the analysis thought was left undone. `synthesize_round` has always been asked for `should_stop` and `stop_reason` and `RoundResult` had no fields for them, so a parse dropped both and reports could not distinguish "the science concluded" from "the round budget ran out" (they are now recorded; convergence remains bookkeeping-driven precisely because the measurement above shows the verdict is False almost every round). Both, plus the audit summary and the pass outcome, now reach `_pi_synthesis`'s bioinformatics context (`workflow/synthesis.py`), on the grounds that an unmet approved step is the strongest available reason to keep a conclusion hedged.
  - **Three defects, two of them introduced here and caught by testing with the real agents rather than mocks.** `sanitize_review_result` built its output with `dict(result)` and so carried the executor's `code_history` — `CodeExecution` objects — into a dict that is `json.dumps`-ed into the PI's re-assessment prompt, pushed over the websocket and written into `session.json`; the PI call raised `TypeError: Object of type CodeExecution is not JSON serializable` and silently degraded to `partial`. Mock-based tests could not see it because a stub PI does not serialize its input; it is now a whitelist of transport-safe keys plus a test that `json.dumps` the whole meta and the stored records. `METHOD_SIGNATURES` listed the bare word `regulon` as a pySCENIC identifier — but decoupler's input network *is* called a regulon set, so "Run decoupler run_ulm on the collected regulons" demanded two families and reported a satisfied step as unmet; identifiers are now tools and functions only, which is the same lesson as rc5's deleted coverage score in miniature. `_clean_list` sliced to the cap before dropping blanks, so empty strings a model emitted consumed task budget (`max_tasks=3` over `["", "  ", real]` yielded one task).
  - **Known and not fixed here.** The audit only sees steps whose text names a tool, so its coverage depends on how the planner phrased the step, and `METHOD_SIGNATURES` is a hand-maintained list that a new tool silently escapes. `pseudotime` is still in the `trajectory` family and is half prose — kept because it also appears as an `obs` column name in real code, so it usually matches, but it is the residual case of the `regulon` class of error. The notebook-restore path constructs `CodeExecution` without a round number (`agents/bioinformatics_agent.py:1073`), and `executed_method_families` disables round filtering when either side is 0, so restored cells vouch for every round — the audit errs toward `completed`, i.e. it under-reports gaps rather than inventing them. The worst case for the total budget is all steps running to their limit (7.7h at the defaults); lower `post_execution_review_total_timeout_seconds` to cap it. `post_execution_reviews` is excluded from `to_api_dict` (no frontend consumer — verified by grepping `frontend/src`), so it reaches the user through the websocket message and the report only, and the UI has a trigger for a supplementary request but nothing that displays the audit. The duplicated tests in `test_snapshot_v2_runtime_edges.py` that rc5 flagged are still not deduplicated, and during this work another concurrent session appended 236 lines to that same file in the shared checkout; those lines are deliberately **not** part of this commit.
  - **Verification is by test only — this feature has not been exercised on a real session end to end**, unlike the replay used for rc5, and that gap is the main risk in this entry: the trigger predicate, the budget derivation and the merge are pinned by tests, but no production run has yet produced a supplementary pass. 52 new backend tests (23 over the pure audit/gate/bounding layer, 22 over the orchestration contracts — audit writes status, the pass is paid for once per execution, neither can fail the round — and 7 driving the real `PIAgent` and `BioinformaticsAgent` with only the LLM and the kernel stubbed, which is what caught both introduced defects above). Full backend suite: 3645 passed, 79 skipped, with the same one pre-existing failure as rc5 (`test_citation_integrity.py::test_lfc_shrink_is_documented_with_its_required_argument`), confirmed pre-existing by running it in a detached worktree at rc5's commit. Frontend: `tsc --noEmit` clean, 31 files / 211 tests passing.

- `0.36.8-rc5` — The rc4 retention trigger measured against real runs and removed; direction payload extended past the rounds that have a candidate; quality pass over rc2-rc4 (2026-07-29):
  - **rc4's retention trigger was calibrated by reasoning about what a loss looks like, and the reasoning was wrong.** It re-prompted whenever a revision dropped more than a handful of the terms its sources supplied. Measured afterwards against all 45 real plan revisions on disk: the retention ratio does not separate a revision that dropped the study's mechanism vocabulary (0.51 / 0.62 / 0.76) from one that kept it (0.42 - 1.00) — the three known positives sit inside the negative distribution — and typical missing counts run to the hundreds, so a threshold of five fired on 43 of 45. In its one production run (rc4 image, 2026-07-29, logs since lost to a container recreation) it fired on 5 of 5 revisions and recovered nothing on all 5, at one model call each. Of the 75 reported terms those logs preserved — each 40-term report is truncated to 15 before logging, so 37% of them — about a third are imperative prose (`consider`, `clarify`, `keep`) carried in verbatim from the PI's step-level modifications, which the comparison source concatenates: the same mistake rc4 fixed for the objective prose and missed on another source. This is the second time in this release series that a check written from first principles had to be rebuilt against measurements (`0.36.8-rc1`, "Gate triggers were rebuilt against real data").
  - **Narrowing it was rejected, then the scoring half was deleted outright (`backend/utils/plan_retention.py` → `backend/utils/text_coverage.py`)**: none of the three known positives has a resolvable direction candidate, so a trigger narrowed to the direction's vocabulary has zero measurable positives left to calibrate against — an unverifiable check does not belong on a production path. Keeping it as a log line was also rejected: every session already persists `plan_dialogue_history`, so the number is recomputable offline whenever a real calibration becomes possible, and a live enforcement-shaped API invites the next reader to re-wire it to the verdict this entry explains does not work. What survives is `uncovered_items`, the coverage helper the module was actually being used for, with the file renamed to match. Removing the score also removed its plumbing from the planner — three private methods, a `priority_text` ranking contract, and a `Tuple[str, DirectionContext]` return whose second element one caller discarded.
  - **The payload had to survive past the rounds that have a candidate (`backend/utils/direction_payload.py`)**: only directions taken out of the exploration report carry a candidate object. Later, a direction is generated from the previous round's next steps or from an integration gate and has none, so keying the payload and the coverage check on the candidate left both silent for nearly half of a long run — over the 85 planning events in the 22 sessions on disk as of 2026-07-29, a candidate resolved for 45. `resolve_direction_context()` falls back to the previous round result, which already states what the next round owes: its next steps are the asks, and its mechanism programs name the entities implicated. Nothing new is generated, so this costs no model call and changes no agent contract. Two distinctions matter. Next steps scoped to any agent other than bioinformatics — pathology, biochemistry and literature alike — plus a mechanism program's `missing_evidence` and `alternative_explanations`, are routed to notes: shown to the planner, never coverage-checked, because a plan cannot run a stain and checking one manufactures a permanent false positive. The cut is coarse: every one of the 2,083 next steps on disk carries an agent prefix, so 1,510 (72.5%) become notes against 573 checkable requirements, and literature — not a wet-lab scope — is the largest demoted bucket. And the walk goes backwards through the results rather than taking the last: an integration round yields a `RoundResult` with no next steps and no programs, so keying on the immediate predecessor goes blank for every direction round that follows one. With both, payload resolution goes from 45 of 85 planning events — 47 once the recorded-index lookup below is added — to 85 of 85. **Known limitation of the fallback**: all 38 events that use it inherit from a round that pursued a *different* direction (35 from two rounds back, 3 from one), so a plan for direction B is coverage-checked against direction A's leftover next steps. That is a new false-positive surface in the bullet whose purpose was removing one, and it is why the fallback feeds an advisory message rather than a gate.
  - **The selection-to-candidate link was already persisted; rc4 recomputed it fuzzily (`backend/utils/direction_payload.py`)**: `map_selections()` resolves each selected direction to its candidate index at gate time and stores it as `SelectionEntry.candidate_index` beside the frozen candidate list in `direction_selection_records`. rc4 ignored that and matched on direction text, with a containment fallback that can bind a *different* candidate's uncertainties and entities to a substring or edited direction — a payload that then enters both planning prompts and drives a user-facing coverage warning, so a wrong attachment is silent. Resolution now reads the recorded index first. On the sessions on disk this buys exactness rather than coverage: 21 of 22 ran PI-decide and hold no `selections` at all, the only 6 indexed selections sit in one session, and replaying all 85 planning events the index changes the outcome for 2 — both integration-gate selections whose candidate the exploration report no longer holds. It also does not rescue an edited direction: `map_selections` derives the index by exact equality, so an edit yields `candidate_index=None` and falls back to the containment match anyway. Bringing the PI-decide path through `map_selections` is the remaining half of this fix and is not done here.
  - **One plan-step renderer instead of two (`backend/utils/formatting.py`)**: `render_plan_steps` moved out of the bioinformatics planner into shared formatting, and `PIAgent.review_bioinfo_plan` now calls it in place of its own copy, which used the same labels, indentation and field order but always emitted empty fields, printed `Depends on: []`, and carried no per-step tag. Both sides of the planning dialogue now read the same rendering, and the PI's copy — previously unbounded — inherits the shrink-to-fit budget.
